# V3.00 — Catalog expansion roadmap

**2026-08-05.** Candidate nodes for the nodegraph v2 catalog, drawn from Nikon NIS-Elements GA3,
Fiji/ImageJ, CellProfiler, scikit-image/napari, Imaris and the other commercial 3D suites, the
tracking / mechanobiology / deep-learning literature, and Blender's own node systems — fit-scored
against the engine source, deduplicated, put through three adversarial critics, and sequenced into
work packages.

> **Caveat.** Provenance was verified by web survey on 2026-08-04; **every third-party dependency
> must be re-checked before it is added** (the surveys confirmed existence and behaviour, not
> current maintenance status). Fit classifications were made by agents reading the engine source,
> then re-derived by a second adversarial pass; the load-bearing ones in section 1 were verified by
> *executing* the engine, and say so. Nothing here has been built. The `value` scores are
> judgements about this lab's work, not measurements.

## 1. The thesis

### 1.1 The field system is built, wired into 31 nodes, and has no producer

`nodegraph/field.py` implements a complete deferred per-element expression IR — `Const` (`:83`),
`Attr` (`:88`), `Input` (`:96`), `BinOp` (`:101`), `UnaryOp` (`:108`), `Where` (`:114`), eleven
binary and six unary operators (`:123-131`), a non-materializing `VirtualArray` (`:40-78`), a
windowed evaluator (`:172-219`), and a memo namespace disjoint from the image tile key (`:248-317`).

Everything downstream of a producer already works. The engine hands every compute a shared
`FieldCache` (`engine.py:336, 587, 757`) and deliberately routes a field payload past `params` so
the consumer can evaluate it per element (`engine.py:263-318`). `sockets.can_connect` states
explicitly that field-ness never blocks a value wire (`sockets.py:97-117`). The GUI already paints
the diamond (`nodelab_v2/node_item.py:435-441`) and labels the socket `field · float` (`:238`).
`analysis.threshold` already consumes one end-to-end (`threshold.py:309-310, 367, 397-407`).

What does not exist is anything that **creates** a field. Grepping the IR constructors across
`nodegraph/catalog/` returns zero hits. The only value outputs among all 72 nodes are
`flow.iterate`'s sweep variables (`iterate.py:291-293`) and the internal, palette-hidden
`flow.advance` (`advance.py:61`) — both plain scalars. There is no Value, Attribute, Math, Compare,
Map Range or Switch node. **The value half of the graph does not exist.**

That absence is the root cause of duplication already in the tree:

| Duplicated concept | Copies | Where |
|---|---|---|
| threshold-method menu to skimage function | 2 real | `threshold.py:25, 323-325`; `segment.py:84, 126-127` |
| statistics-population "scope" vocabulary | 2 (+1 correctly distinct) | `threshold.py:32, 44-58`; `normalize.py:267` |
| reducer choice tuple | 6 | `transfer_domain.py:26`, `transfer_structure.py:46,48`, `reduce_scalar.py:33`, `bridges.py:37`, `stack.py:61`, `zproject.py:28` |
| min/max object-size filter | 3 unit systems | `segment.py:750-786`; `histogram_threshold.py:519-539`; `particles.py:144` |
| comma-separated vocab parser | 4 near-identical | `measure.py:47, ~83`; `object_metrics.py:40`; `object_field.py:39` |
| "what a region table contains" | 3 definitions | `histogram_threshold.py:331-336`; `segment.py:49,63`; `measure.py:29,68` |

And it is the root cause of the workflow gap the surveys converged on hardest. **`table.filter` —
"measure a property, then act on it" — was proposed independently by 7 of the 10 surveys**, the
most over-proposed node in the exercise; `value.math` by 6. Today `analysis.measure` and
`analysis.object_metrics` write columns that *nothing can act on*: you can compute sphericity and
you cannot remove the non-spherical objects. Two shipped nodes are already hand-fused field
expressions — `analysis.threshold_local` (`:22-34`) is *(local mean) − offset*, then compare, and
`enhance.flatten_field` (`:65-68`) is four hard-coded `BinOp`s over a background field.

### 1.2 Correction: the structure-domain block does not exist

An earlier draft of this document asserted that filtering a structure table by a field predicate
needs a `field.py` change, because `field.evaluate` raises `NotImplementedError` on a
structure-domain `Attr`. **That was wrong, and it mattered — it was the stated gate on the whole
table layer.** The raise is only for a **cross-domain** read. `field.py:186-189` returns
`layer.values` whenever `field.domain is ctx.domain`, *before* any lattice check:

```python
if field.domain is ctx.domain:
    if ctx.window is not None:
        return layer.values[ctx.slicer(field.domain)]
    return layer.values
```

Verified by execution against a LABEL table with `area` and `mean_intensity` columns:

| Expression evaluated on `FieldContext(ds, Domain.LABEL, ax)` | Result |
|---|---|
| `Attr(LABEL,'area')` | `[10. 80. 120. 30.]` |
| `BinOp('>', Attr(LABEL,'area'), Const(50.))` | `[False True True False]` |
| `… AND BinOp('>', Attr(LABEL,'mean_intensity'), Const(100.))` | `[False False True False]` |
| `Where(pred, Const(1.), Const(0.))` | `[0. 1. 1. 0.]` |
| `Attr(LABEL,'area')` on `Domain.VOXEL` | `NotImplementedError` — cross-domain only |

A second premise was equally load-bearing and equally false: that a curve, spectrum or histogram
needs a new domain to live on. `Dataset.with_attribute` shape-checks **only lattice domains**
(`dataset.py:200-210`), and `StructureTable.columns` is an unvalidated mapping. Verified: a 20-row
`msd_curve` table with free-form `lag_s` / `msd_um2` / `n_pairs` columns is accepted on a `T=5`
dataset, while a wrongly-shaped PLANE layer is still refused (`ValueError: plane attribute 'bad' has
shape (3, 3), expected (1, 5, 1)`).

**Consequence.** `table.filter`, `table.computed_column` and `track.msd` — three of the four
highest-value candidates in the set — are `drop-in`, not `new-core`. What they wait on is a producer
*node*, which is catalog work. The `field.py:208-210` package that gated the entire table layer
dissolves; it is needed only to rasterize a per-object value back to voxels, which
`transform.transfer_structure` already does by another route.

### 1.3 A third of the set is not nodes

The overlap critics were asked not merely to kill duplicates but to decide, for each candidate, what
it *becomes* if the overlap is removed — a mode on an existing node, a smaller node, or nothing.
All 269 got a verdict ([Appendix E](#appendix-e---overlap-disposition-all-269)):

| Verdict | n | Share |
|---|---|---|
| `distinct` — build as proposed | 116 | 43% |
| **`fold-into-mode`** — a Mode/parameter edit on an existing node | **92** | **34%** |
| `rescope` — real residual, smaller node | 57 | 21% |
| `subsumed` — already reachable; dropped | 4 | 1% |

**Only 4 of 269 were genuinely already possible.** The surveys were not proposing things that
exist; they were proposing them at the wrong *granularity*. Four shipped nodes absorb a third of
the folds — `analysis.segment` 12, `analysis.measure` 9, `analysis.object_metrics` 9,
`analysis.edt` 5 — mostly as vocabulary extensions to constants that already exist
(`_SEGMENT_METHODS` at `segment.py:81`, `_MEASURE_SHAPE` at `measure.py:68`,
`_OBJECT_METRIC_COLUMNS` at `object_metrics.py:25`). Ten more hosts are themselves candidates: a
secondary dedupe no blind survey could have seen.

### 1.4 Nothing in the set guards the numbers the app already prints

The completeness critic found the blind spot none of the ten surveys could have revealed about
itself: **the candidate set is entirely additive.** Absent from all 269 candidates, all 205
rejections and all 72 shipped nodes — and each verified in the source:

| Gap | Evidence | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| Refractive-index axial scaling | — | Every µm³, sphericity, axial distance, EDT and DVC z-displacement is off by the focal-shift factor when immersion and specimen RI differ. The normal case in live-cell aqueous samples. |
| Immersion `n` hard-coded | `_shared/psf.py:43` — `sz_um = 0.66*lam_um*1.5/(na*na)` | The axial PSF assumes oil for every objective, so `enhance.deconvolve` and ZS-DeconvNet's training PSF are wrong on water and air. |
| Real per-frame timestamps discarded | `selftest.py:11425` records the original defect verbatim: *"Every velocity in the app was therefore µm/frame wearing a µm/s label."* The fix was a **median** `dt_s`, which still discards per-frame truth. | Every µm/s and 1/s in `object_metrics`, `track.*` and `temporal_gain` assumes a uniform interval a 49-position run does not have. |
| Anisotropic DVC subsets | `dvc_field.py:211` — `InInt("subset_size", unit="px")` | A cubic subset in voxels is not cubic in µm on an anisotropic stack. |
| Z-clipped and border-touching objects | — | An object clipped by the top or bottom of a stack thinner than the cells is measured as if whole — the dominant bias in reported volume and sphericity. |
| Saturation inside measured objects | — | No node checks whether the pixels it is averaging are clipped. |
| Derived parameters are invisible | `analysis.threshold` emits only a mask | The app's auto-chosen Otsu/Li levels, derived sigmas and derived PSFs are unauditable and unplottable. |
| `reducers.py` has no dispersion | `REDUCERS` = `count first max mean median min sigma_clip sum trimmed_mean` | No `std`, `var`, `range`, `iqr` or percentile anywhere — silently limiting `analysis.measure`, `analysis.reduce_scalar` and both Transfer nodes at once. |

The critic's structural observation is sharper than any individual item: **convergence is
anti-correlated with correctness.** Every `conv >= 4` candidate is generic plumbing that every
survey needed as a precondition. Every domain-specific correctness item is `conv 1` — as is
`core.batch_files`, in a lab whose data is 49-position plates. Ten independent surveys of prior art
converge on what tools *have*, not on what a given lab's numbers *need*.

Four shipped nodes are verified stubs: `align.drift` (**88 lines, zero parameter sockets**,
hard-coded mid-plane/channel-0/t=0 reference, lateral-only — axial focus drift over a multi-hour 3D
run is corrected by nothing in the app or the set), `enhance.wavelet_denoise` (**43 lines, zero
params**), `analysis.threshold_local` (hard-coded gaussian, 2D only), and `analysis.roi_mask` (all
drawn shapes collapse into one mask, so "draw N regions and trace each over time" is
inexpressible).

And the deepest Blender analogue nobody proposed: **a per-position (`m`) zone.** There is one for
`t` (`zone.frame`) and none for the acquisition's own outer axis, despite MULTIPOINT being one of
the eleven domains. On a 49-position plate, every per-well decision — a threshold picked per well, a
per-position drift reference, resume-after-failure at position 31 — is currently inexpressible.

### 1.5 There is still no output layer

Per `sockets.py:113-114` the only wire type is DATASET, and a compute must return a `Dataset`
(`memo.py:145, 172`). **There is no file-output node in `nodegraph/catalog/` at all** — the only
file writers in the tree are `nodegraph/checkpoint.py` (a bake, driven by the GUI's `io.dock`) and
`StructureTable.to_arrow` (`structure.py:69`), neither a node.

The architecture critics found that fifteen candidates were independently rejected or deferred on
this basis, and that the reasoning conflates two things: **a compute's return value must be
wireable, but a compute may freely write files as a side effect** — `enhance.zs_deconvnet` already
declares `path_kind="save_file"` and writes a trained model. Likewise a figure does not need a new
artifact kind: a plot is an image, and an image is what a Dataset carries, with a `meta_transform`
setting the render's axes to constants. So `io.write_*`, `plot.*` and `io.rois` are `drop-in` after
all. This is the single largest block the corrections unlocked.

## 3. Engine constraints that bound every proposal

**The registration surface is closed.** `define_node` (`registry.py:986-1013`) accepts exactly
`op_key, label, category, inputs, outputs, modes, description, granularity, kernel_axes,
footprint_mode, meta_transform, supports_2d, supports_true_3d, three_d_fallback, reads_domains,
reads_domains_by_mode, adds_domains, extra_layers`. A new declaration means editing `NodeSpec`
(`registry.py:356-494`), `define_node`, and every consumer.

**Granularity has five members** (`registry.py:41-50`): `TILEABLE`, `WHOLE_PLANE`, `WHOLE_VOLUME`,
`WHOLE_SERIES`, `MULTI_VIEW`. Two consequences:

- `WHOLE_SERIES` and `MULTI_VIEW` **fall off the lazy path** — `_map_image` requires granularity in
  {TILEABLE, WHOLE_PLANE, WHOLE_VOLUME} (`map_image.py:98-101`), so anything else eagerly realizes
  the full 6-D `(M,T,Z,C,Y,X)` float64 array (`:107`) unless the node hand-rolls a provider.
- **But a node that reads only tables never enters `_map_image` at all**, so its granularity
  declaration is inert and `WHOLE_SERIES` costs nothing. The architecture critics found this
  exemption applies to a large share of the candidates that looked expensive
  ([Appendix F.2](#f2-class-kept-declaration-corrected-29)).
- Granularity is advisory to the engine: it only sets `ctx.granularity`/`ctx.is_volume`
  (`engine.py:432-435, 727-728`). All routing is done by the compute. **A misdeclared granularity is
  silent.**

**`meta_transform` can rewrite** axis sizes, any metadata key, `pixel_size_um`, `bit_depth`,
`origin_um`, and per-channel/per-position lists (`metadata.py:106, 269-578`). It **cannot** see any
input but the primary (`propagate_meta` passes only `dpreds[0]`, `metadata.py:612`), touch `domains`
or `layer_names` (layered on afterwards, `:620-692`), read pixels, raise anything but `ValueError`,
or see a wired param value (`engine.py:751`).

**Transfers.** Lattice-to-lattice is *generated* (`transfer.py:207-288`). Structure hops are
*explicit bridges* — 16 registered pairs (`transfer.py:107-140`), 12 with functions
(`bridges.py:345-358`), surfaced as 15 ordered pairs by `transform.transfer_structure`. Bridges
serve only the **generic** path: `execute_transfer` raises on any BridgeStep (`transfer.py:279-284`),
so a self-contained compute that reads two label rasters off one Dataset needs no bridge at all —
which is why `relate.parent` turned out to be drop-in. **MESH is isolated in the transfer graph**:
no `register_bridge` mentions `Domain.MESH`, so `plan_transfer` raises for every Mesh pair.

**`reads_domains`/`adds_domains` are advisory.** They drive the GUI's domain rail and
missing-domain chips (`metadata.py:620-627`). Nothing at pull time enforces them.

### What the engine cannot currently express

Revised after the architecture pass. Several entries from the earlier draft were removed because
they were not true.

1. **New socket types.** `SocketType` (`sockets.py:24-32`) is a closed enum, `_CONVERSIONS`
   (`:54-64`) a closed table, `SOCKET_COLOR` (`:41-50`) must have an entry or the GUI theme
   KeyErrors. **No list socket** — hence comma-separated STRINGs with `vocab=`. `MENU` exists with
   no producer or consumer.
2. **Per-element writes into an existing structure table.** No mutate-in-place path. A node re-emits
   the whole table via `with_structure` and must carry `z_kind` forward by hand or it silently
   reverts to `"subpixel"` (`measure.py:284-286, 333-341`).
3. **N-ary Dataset inputs, in general.** `InDataset(multi=True)` works (`engine.py:736-750`), but
   `propagate_meta` reads only `dpreds[0]`, so a genuinely N-ary node cannot predict its own
   envelope — why `channel.merge` marks `c` and `z` UNKNOWN.
4. **A node creating a domain instance in a domain it did not declare.** `adds_domains` is per-type
   while an instance's domain can be a per-instance Mode value; the `transfer_*` nodes announce via
   `extra_layers` and the domain rail is then silently wrong.
5. **Random access across frames.** No granularity for it, no provider primitive beyond
   `read_region(...)` (`provider.py:55-58`).
6. **Cycles.** Rejected in `graph.py:140-161` and `engine.py:659-661`. The only escapes are
   `NON_DAG_KINDS = {"back","driver"}` consumed by `zones.unroll` and `iterate` before the engine
   sees the graph — and zones **unroll**, so cost is linear in iteration count.
7. **Lazy calibration reads.** `ReadContext.freeze()` (`engine.py:83, 781`) makes a `ctx.calib` call
   from inside a returned lazy closure a hard `RuntimeError`.
8. **Cross-domain fields.** `field.evaluate` raises on an `Attr` whose domain differs from the
   consuming domain when either side is non-lattice (`field.py:208-210`), and on windowed coarsening
   (`:204-206`). **Same-domain structure reads work** — see [1.2](#12-correction-the-structure-domain-block-does-not-exist).

**Not limits, contrary to the earlier draft:** a compute may write files as a side effect (proven by
`enhance.zs_deconvnet`); a figure may be returned as a Dataset image; a structure table may carry
arbitrary row counts and free-form columns; field expressions need no serializer, being runtime
payloads reconstructed from `op_key` + params.

**Still uncertain:** `supports_2d`/`supports_true_3d`/`three_d_fallback` have no runtime consumer
found; `SocketSpec.domain` is stored and never read; `MetaEnvelope.layers` is an acknowledged stub;
`kernel_axes` is resolved into `EvalContext` and nothing reads it at runtime; `unit="um2"`/`"um3"`
are named in the skill but `to_pixels_v2` (`_shared/units.py:14-23`) returns them unchanged, so a
node using them must convert by hand.

---

## 4. The `field=True` hazard, and why it is Wave 0

The most important operational finding, and it inverts the obvious build order. It was reached
independently twice: once by the grounding pass, and again by an overlap critic that had not seen
the grounding pass and rescoped `core.field_wire` (v10 to 8) to *"field socket honesty sweep +
field serialization."*

`_in_value()` at `registry.py:539-540` declares **`field: bool = True`**. Field-ability is the
*default* for `InFloat/InInt/InBool/InVector/InColor/InString`, and for `OutValue` (`:577`). So
**31 nodes declare 72 field-able value sockets** — mostly the default not overridden, not a
decision.

Exactly **one** compute evaluates a field: `analysis.threshold`. Every other node reads
`ctx.params.get(name, fallback)`. And `engine._with_driven_params` deliberately does **not** fold a
field payload into `params` (`engine.py:263-318`, documented at `:303-308`).

Verified on `enhance.gaussian`:

- `gaussian.py:38` declares `InFloat("sigma", …, field=True, kernel_param=True)`.
- `gaussian.py:24` reads `float(ctx.params.get("sigma", 0.5))`.
- So a field wired to Sigma would (a) demote the footprint from TILEABLE to plane unit via
  `_kernel_field_varies` (`map_image.py:26-42, 77-78`), and (b) **be silently discarded**, blurring
  at the default 0.5 µm.

The same holds for the other ~70. The suite already forbids exactly this "`field=True` would be a
lie" defect on three specific nodes (`selftest.py:2291, 3653, 7865`) but **never checks it
catalog-wide**. It is unreachable today only because nothing produces a field.

**Consequence: shipping a Math node first would activate a silent-wrong-answer bug across ~71
sockets at once.** The producer must not land before the consumer contract is honest.

---

## 5. The waves

Ordered by dependency and leverage. Sizes are S (hours), M (a day), L (several days), XL (a week+),
on top of the mandatory per-node cost in [§6](#6-contention-points-and-merge-discipline).

**The ledger after the critics.** 269 candidates became: 116 build as proposed, 57 build reduced,
92 fold into 44 existing nodes as modes, 4 dropped; the architecture pass moved 59 into a cheaper
fit class and corrected 29 more, leaving **201 drop-in, 41 small-core, 27 new-core** (from 150 /
61 / 56); the completeness pass added 16 nodes and 17 deepenings. Net: **~189 new nodes and ~109
modifications to existing nodes.**

### Wave 0 — Make `field=True` true (no new nodes)

Before any producer exists, every socket advertising field-ability must honour it or stop
advertising it, and the suite must enforce that catalog-wide. Hard prerequisite for Wave 1.

| id | Title | Changes | Files | Prereq | Gate | Parallel | Size |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| W0-P1 | Field-honesty audit | Classify all 72 field-able sockets: *honours*, *should honour*, *must set `field=False`*. Change nothing. | audit only | — | the written classification, reviewed | yes | M |
| W0-P2 | Catalog-wide honesty gate | New selftest: every `is_field=True` socket's compute must read it via `ctx.input(name)`, else declare `field=False`. Generalises `selftest.py:2291, 3653, 7865`. | `selftest.py` | W0-P1 | new test green; whole suite green | **serial** | M |
| W0-P3 | Demote the lying sockets | `field=False` on everything in the *must* bucket. | ~29 catalog files | W0-P2 | full suite; only expected memo re-key | yes | M |
| W0-P4 | Shared field-lift helper | `_shared/fields.py`: `lift(ctx, name, default)` and an `evaluate_windowed` wrapper factoring `threshold.py:397-407`. | new `_shared/fields.py`; refactor `threshold.py` | W0-P2 | `threshold` bit-identical | **serial** with W0-P5 | M |
| W0-P5 | Promote the honest consumers | Teach the highest-value kernel params to evaluate: `enhance.gaussian` sigma, `analysis.threshold_local` block_size/offset, `transform.grow_points` radius, `detect.spots` radii. | 4 catalog files | W0-P4 | per-node test that a wired field changes output; footprint demotion asserted | yes | L |

### Wave 1 — The value spine and the measure-then-act loop (22 nodes)

The producers, plus the three table nodes the corrections turned drop-in. Per the grounding trace a
Math node needs **no engine, registry, sockets, field or GUI change** — only a catalog module, an
entry in the ordered `MODULES` tuple, selftest coverage and a MANUAL row.

The sink is where the work is: `Dataset.with_layer` needs a full canonical-shape ndarray with no
lazy path (`dataset.py:200-228`), so the proven pattern is `spill.dense_output` to windowed
`FieldContext` loop to `seal()` to `with_layer` (`threshold.py:378-380, 418`). **A producer without
a sink emits a value nothing can terminate**, so W1-P1 and W1-P2 land together.

| id | Title | Nodes | Files | Prereq | Gate | Parallel | Size |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| W1-P1 | Field producers | `value.constant` `value.math` `value.compare` `value.boolean_math` | new `catalog/value/*`; `catalog/__init__.py` | W0-P2 | selftest pulls `Math -> threshold` end-to-end | **serial** on `__init__.py` | L |
| W1-P2 | The sink | `table.computed_column` (lattice **and** structure targets — both work per [1.2](#12-correction-the-structure-domain-block-does-not-exist)) | new module | W1-P1 | a Math result is readable as a layer and a column; survives save/load | **serial** | M |
| W1-P3 | Attribute reads | `value.named_attribute` `value.domain_size` `value.index` `value.position` | new modules | W1-P1 | layer picker resolves; cross-lattice `Attr` exercised | yes | M |
| W1-P4 | **Filter** | `core.row_subset` (shared re-emit + LUT relabel, factored from `_segment_size_filter`) then `table.filter` | new `_shared/rows.py`; new module | W1-P1 | subsumes `segment`'s size filter on an equivalent graph; `z_kind` survives; per-clause counts reported | **serial** internally | M |
| W1-P5 | Range and vector utilities | `value.map_range` `value.random` `value.vector_math` | new modules; `field.py` for `clamp`/`floor`/`ceil`/`round`/`pow`/`%` | W1-P1 | new operators unit-tested | **serial** on `field.py` | L |

Dropped from this wave versus the earlier draft: `value.separate_xyz` (subsumed — vector data is
already stored as separate scalar columns, since `with_attribute` admits no vector-valued lattice
layer) and `flow.bake` (subsumed — `io.dock` is an ordinary graph node and several may be placed).

### Wave C — Correctness (17 items). Runs in parallel with Wave 1 and outranks Wave 3

Everything in [1.4](#14-nothing-in-the-set-guards-the-numbers-the-app-already-prints). These are not
new capabilities; they are the difference between the app's existing numbers being right and being
plausible. **This wave did not exist before the completeness critic ran**, and on the evidence it
should precede most of the measurement depth in Wave 3.

| WC-P3 | Object validity | `analysis.border_bias` — flag/discard LABEL rows touching y/x edges and, separately, z=0 / z=nz-1; unbiased counting-frame mode | W1-P4 | a synthetic stack thinner than its objects reports the bias | yes | M |
| WC-P4 | Saturation and dispersion | saturation fraction inside measured objects; add `std`/`var`/`range`/`iqr`/percentile to `nodegraph/reducers.py` — **one edit that reaches `analysis.measure`, `analysis.reduce_scalar` and both Transfer nodes** | — | `reducer_docs` coverage gate; four nodes gain the options with no per-node edit | **serial** (`reducers.py`) | M |
| WC-P5 | Auditable decisions | `core.derived_param_out` — every derived or data-chosen parameter emitted as a layer on the domain its scope implies: `analysis.threshold`'s level per `scope`, `multiotsu`'s K−1 levels, derived sigmas and PSFs | — | the chosen Otsu level is readable, plottable and sweepable | yes | L |
| WC-P6 | Interrogate the source | `qc.acquisition_integrity` (declared vs decodable frame count, per-position counts, timestamp monotonicity, stage-log consistency) · `qc.nyquist` (lateral/axial oversampling from NA, λ, n) | — | an aborted/short ND2 is flagged rather than measured | yes | L |

## 6. Contention points and merge discipline

Seven files are wanted by nearly every package.

| File | Who touches it | Discipline |
|---|---|---|
| `nodegraph/catalog/__init__.py` | **every** new node | `MODULES` is an **ordered, load-bearing** tuple gated by `scripts/_catalog_snapshot.py` against `scripts/catalog_baseline.json`. **Append only, never insert.** One integrator applies these in package order. |
| `nodegraph/selftest.py` | every package | 15,295 lines and the gate. Append a test function plus one `main()` registration line. Serialize; never reformat. |
| `MANUAL.md` §15 | every node | Append rows; the counts are in the headings (`Enhancement (19)`) — update them. |
| `nodegraph/reducers.py` | WC-P4, W2-P7 | One edit reaches four shipped nodes. Strictly serial, and it must go through `reducer_docs` so the prose cannot drift (`zproject.py:105-120` hand-writes its own today — fix while there). |
| `nodegraph/field.py` | W1-P5 | Strictly serial. Only the operator additions; the structure branch is **not** needed. |
| `nodegraph/registry.py` | any new socket/spec field | Serial, own review — it widens the closed surface in §3. |
| `CodeLog/Updates/CHANGELOG.md` | every package | Append-only, one entry per package. |

The four vocabulary packages (W3-P0a…d) are each **one file** and each absorb 5–12 candidates, so
they serialize *internally* but parallelise perfectly against each other and against everything else.

**The memo consequence.** `node_recipe_hash` keys on `op_key + params + upstream hashes + upstream
revisions + code fingerprint`, and `revision.code_fingerprint(op_key)` is read per lookup
(`engine.py:704-718`). Per-node memo granularity is the entire reason for one-module-per-node and
the five import rules (`selftest.py:10275`) — in particular **no node module may import another node
module**. Violating it collapses memo granularity silently: a measured 76 to ~1497
over-invalidations (`selftest.py:10292-10294`). Shared code goes in `_shared/`.

**Free vs not free.** `description`, `choice_docs`, `path_kind` and the `pick_*` family are
presentation-only and memo-neutral (`selftest.py:9938-9944`). Adding a socket or Mode, or changing
`granularity`/`kernel_axes`/`meta_transform`, re-keys.

---

## 7. Risk register

| Risk | Assessment | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| **Shipping a field producer before Wave 0** | Severe and certain: silent-wrong-answer behaviour on ~71 sockets at once ([§4](#4-the-fieldtrue-hazard-and-why-it-is-wave-0)). | Wave 0 gates Wave 1; W0-P2's catalog-wide test is the enforcement. |
| **Trusting a `fit` classification** | The fit pass was wrong on 88 of 269 and wrong in one direction *every time* — 59 candidates were deferred as harder than they are, including four of the five most valuable. | Re-derive fit at build time. Treat `new-core` as a hypothesis; the architecture pass found most of them were a missing *node*, not a missing capability. |
| **`WHOLE_SERIES` over-declaration** | 189 of 269 declared it; most surveyors did not know it drops off the lazy path. Many are inert (table-only nodes never enter `_map_image`) and many are real. | Re-derive granularity per node against `map_image.py:98-107`. Appendix A's value is a hint. |
| **Building descriptors before correctness** | The set is entirely additive; ~8 verified defects affect numbers the app already prints ([1.4](#14-nothing-in-the-set-guards-the-numbers-the-app-already-prints)). Convergence is anti-correlated with correctness, so the survey process actively hid these. | Wave C runs alongside Wave 1 and precedes Wave 3. Do not let `conv` drive priority. |
| **torch** | Not a dependency; CPU-only Windows, no CUDA. | Do not add it. `model.pixel_classifier`/`object_classifier` are random-forest, scikit-learn only. Prefer ONNX Runtime if learned inference is wanted. |
| **TensorFlow ceiling** | CPU-only, 64 GiB allocator cap, no native-Windows CUDA since TF 2.11. | Budget at CPU speed, small tiles, graph mode. Do not plan around GPU. |
| **New numerical kernels (Wave 5)** | Project memory: a green selftest plus a plausible correlation has twice hidden a materially wrong field. | Replicate the upstream synthetic suite as a repo-side bench; distrust its tolerances; score against golden output; check both orientations. |
| **Java-bridge dependencies** | Several strong Fiji candidates are Java. | Prefer a Python equivalent; a JVM dependency is a separate explicit decision. |
| **A twelfth domain** | Four XL proposals (`group`/`profile`/`pair`/`curve`), each gating other work. | All deferred — a structure table already carries arbitrary rows and free-form columns ([1.2](#12-correction-the-structure-domain-block-does-not-exist)). Require a package that provably cannot be built against the eleven. |
| **Vocabulary packages are single-file bottlenecks** | W3-P0a…d touch one file each and absorb 5–12 candidates. | They serialize internally by design. Do not split one across subagents; do run all four concurrently. |

---

## 8. Minimum viable slice

Cheaper than the earlier draft, because the three highest-value table nodes turned out to be
drop-in. **Wave 0 complete, W1-P1/P2/P3/P4, WC-P2, WC-P4, and W4-P1:**

| Package | Why |
|---|---|
| W0-P1…P5 | Without it the rest is unsafe. |
| W1-P1 + W1-P2 | Constant/Math/Compare/Boolean plus a sink that writes both layers and columns. |
| W1-P3 | Named Attribute — without it Math has nothing to read. |
| W1-P4 | **Filter**, the node 7 of 10 surveys asked for. Now drop-in. |
| WC-P2 | Real per-frame time. Every µm/s in the app is currently a median approximation. |
| WC-P4 | `std`/`var`/percentile in `reducers.py` — one edit, four shipped nodes gain the options. |
| W4-P1 | Four cheap QC nodes; immediate value on a 49-position plate. |

**What the user gets that they cannot do today:** compute a value from an image or a measurement,
wire it into any parameter, store it as a named column, and **delete the objects that fail a
predicate** — then re-measure. Plus velocities computed from real timestamps rather than a median
interval, dispersion statistics on every existing measurement, and a per-field quality score to
decide what is worth analysing at all.

Roughly 12 packages, ~14 new nodes, and three shared-file edits.

---

## 9. How this was produced

Two agents read the engine source and produced a 64 KB constraints brief. Ten surveys of prior art,
blind to each other, produced 634 proposals. A fit pass rejected 205 and scored 429; a dedupe
collapsed those to 269 canonical candidates, recording how many independent surveys converged on
each. Three adversarial critics then ran over all 269:

| Critic | Shards | Output |
|---|---|---|
| Overlap disposition | 7 | 269 dispositions, full coverage: 116 distinct, 92 fold-into-mode, 57 rescope, 4 subsumed ([App. E](#appendix-e---overlap-disposition-all-269)) |
| Architecture fit | 3 | 88 corrections, all in the easier direction ([App. F](#appendix-f---architecture-corrections-88)) |
| Completeness | 1 | 37 findings: 16 additions, 17 deepenings, 4 flags ([App. G](#appendix-g---completeness-findings-37)) |

**What was verified by execution rather than by reading**, because it was load-bearing and because
an earlier draft of this document got it wrong: structure-domain field predicates
([1.2](#12-correction-the-structure-domain-block-does-not-exist)), arbitrary-length structure tables,
the lattice shape gate still firing, `_shared/psf.py:43`'s hard-coded 1.5, `dvc_field.py:211`'s
`unit="px"`, `align/drift.py`'s zero parameter sockets, `enhance/wavelet_denoise.py`'s 43 lines, and
`reducers.py`'s missing dispersion vocabulary.

**Known remaining weaknesses.** The 269 rows in Appendix A carry their *original* fit and
granularity; Appendix F's corrections are recorded separately rather than merged into them, so read
both. 150 dispositions are `medium` confidence and 6 are `low` — those are the rows to re-examine
first. Appendix G's 16 additions have not been through a fit pass at all. And no candidate anywhere
has been built, so every `drop-in` remains a reading of the code rather than a demonstration.

---

## 10. Sources

Surveyed 2026-08-04 by web search against primary documentation. Re-check before adding any
dependency.

- **Nikon** NIS-Elements General Analysis 3 (GA3) node reference; Advanced Research modules; NIS.ai.
- **Fiji/ImageJ2** and plugins: MorphoLibJ, 3D ImageJ Suite, AnalyzeSkeleton/Skeletonize3D, BoneJ,
  OrientationJ, Directionality, FibrilTool, TWOMBLI, CT-FIRE, Ridge Detection, FeatureJ, Coloc2,
  JACoP, Auto Threshold / Auto Local Threshold, bUnwarpJ, Correct 3D Drift, TrackMate, Bio-Formats,
  Trainable Weka Segmentation, Labkit.
- **CellProfiler** 4.x module set; **scikit-image**; **napari** and its plugin ecosystem.
- **Commercial 3D**: Imaris (Surfaces/Spots/Filaments/Cells, MeasurementPro, Vantage), arivis
  Vision4D, Huygens, Amira/Avizo, Dragonfly, QuPath.
- **Tracking**: TrackMate v7 feature set, trackpy, btrack, Ultrack, LapTrack, Mastodon, Cell
  Tracking Challenge metrics, trackastra.
- **Mechanobiology**: FTTC and regularized/Bayesian traction force microscopy, monolayer stress
  microscopy, OpenPIV/PIVlab conventions, optical flow (Farnebäck, Lucas-Kanade, TV-L1),
  SerialTrack/TPT, the FIDVC/ALDVC family.
- **Deep learning**: Cellpose/Cellpose-SAM, Omnipose, InstanSeg, micro-SAM/SAM2, Mesmer/DeepCell,
  nnU-Net, CARE/CSBDeep, Noise2Void/N2V2, RCAN-3D, DFCAN, SRRF, SQUIRREL, VoxelMorph,
  BioImage Model Zoo, DeepImageJ, ONNX Runtime.
- **Blender**: Geometry Nodes, Shader and Compositor node references, 4.x features (For Each
  Geometry Element zone, Menu Switch, Index Switch, matrix/rotation sockets, gizmos, node tools,
  bake, group interface panels), Node Wrangler.
- **Data/HCS**: OME-NGFF/OME-Zarr, OME-TIFF, plate normalization and Z'-factor/SSMD assay QC,
  CellProfiler Analyst, ilastik object classification.
- **Sample-physics corrections** surfaced by the completeness critic: refractive-index mismatch and
  axial focal shift (Diel, Lichtman & Richardson 2020, *Nat Protoc*); unbiased counting frames and
  border-object bias (stereology); Nyquist sampling for a given NA and λ.

Full per-candidate provenance is in
[Appendix B](#appendix-b---provenance-and-backend-per-candidate).

## Appendix A - Candidate reference (269 nodes)

Every candidate that survived the fit pass and the dedupe, grouped by proposed category and
sorted by value within it. `value` is a 1-10 judgement of worth to this lab, not a
measurement. `conv` is the number of **independent** surveys that proposed the node; `conv`
of 3 or more is the strongest signal in this exercise. `fit` is `drop-in` (buildable with
today's NodeSpec vocabulary), `small-core` (one new Granularity case, reducer, bridge pair or
mode choice) or `new-core` (a new socket type, domain, zone kind, artifact kind, or field
support that does not exist yet).

### A.1 `core-capability` (29)

| op_key | Node | What it does | In -> Out | Gran | Lever | Deps | Eff | Fit | Val | Conv |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `core.field_wire` | Field Wires (diamond sockets, value outputs) | Make the existing field IR wireable end-to-end: allow a value socket to be an OUTPUT carrying a `nodegraph.field.Field` expression tree (not just a resolved number), let field wires connect field-out -> field-in, paint diamond vs round socket shapes per Blender convention, and give every field wire a declared source domain so the GUI can show the automatic lattice-transfer reducer on the wire.... | any (a field is evaluated on the CONSUMING node's domain) -> n/a (value sockets) | TILEABLE | no | none (numpy/scipy/skimage) | L | new-core | 10 | 1 |
| `core.annotation_layer` | Sparse Annotation Layer + Brush | A first-class sparse annotation layer: an integer class raster on the VOXEL domain that is mostly 'unlabelled' (0) and is authored by hand in the viewer with a brush/scribble/polygon/flood tool, stored sparsely (run-length or a coordinate list per class), versioned with the graph, and exposed as a node output so any node can consume it. Includes an eraser, per-class visibility, a 'labelled-pixel... | n/a (authored, not computed) -> A sparse VOXEL integer class layer, plus POINT tables for... | W_PLANE | yes | none (numpy/scipy) | XL | new-core | 9 | 4 |

node. Where several surveys proposed the same node they usually cited different tools; the
clearest single citation is given here and `sources` counts how many were recorded in
`merged_table.json`.

## Appendix C - Declared overlap with the existing 72 (257 nodes)

Read this before building any of these: the surveyor or the fit oracle judged the node to
touch existing behaviour, and the note says what the delta is. A node whose delta turns out
to be one mode belongs on the host node instead.

| op_key | Overlaps / delta |
|---|---|
| `analysis.anisotropy_mil` | analysis.structure_tensor gives a local, gradient-based orientation field; MIL gives a global, boundary-crossing-based fabric tensor and does not need a gradient, so it works on thresholded structures where gradients are step-like. Different estimators of related quantities - worth documenting the difference in choice_docs. |
| `analysis.area_occupied` | analysis.reduce_scalar with domain=label and reducer=count gives an object count and, per its docstring, area in VOXELS not um^2. Could be an extra reducer set there (effort S) rather than a node. |
| `analysis.assemble_3d` | track.objects does the same overlap/IoU association across T, not z, and writes TRACK membership rather than fusing label ids. The maths is close enough to share a kernel, but the output contract is different (one 3D object, not a track). |
| `analysis.bleach_rate` | enhance.temporal_gain already estimates and applies a bleach correction. This is the measurement-only sibling and could be a 'report_only' output on that node (op_key enhance.temporal_gain, effort S) - the honest phrasing is that temporal_gain should expose the fitted rate it already computes. |
| `analysis.coloc` | none. |
| `analysis.coloc_objects` | analysis.object_metrics has n_neighbors/neighbor_dist_mean within one point set; there is no cross-layer object relation node. Distinct from the pixel colocalization node below, which never segments. |
| `analysis.coloc_shift_ccf` | Conceptually adjacent to align.drift (which also cross-correlates) but that node returns a single best shift for registration; this one needs the whole r-vs-lag curve as a readout, and it does not move any pixels. |
| `analysis.coloc_significance` | none. Could be a mode of analysis.colocalize but its cost profile (N x the work) and its own randomization params argue for a separate node. |
| `analysis.compartments` | analysis.boundary_band builds one outward band; mask.ring (proposed) builds shells. Neither reconciles TWO segmentations into co-indexed compartments or applies the largest-nucleus/no-nucleus housekeeping, which is where the real bugs live. |
| `analysis.connected_islands` | analysis.label does connected components on a VOXEL raster with 4/8 or 6/18/26 connectivity. This is the same idea on mesh topology, where the answer genuinely differs. |
| `analysis.curvature` | none. analysis.measure gives 2D eccentricity/perimeter/solidity; nothing computes a boundary derivative. |
| `analysis.eroded_volume_fraction` | analysis.edt gives absolute distance; analysis.boundary_band gives fixed-width absolute bands. Neither normalizes per object, which is the whole point. |
| `analysis.field_profile` | analysis.reduce_scalar collapses a field to one number and analysis.boundary_band selects one shell. This produces the whole binned profile in one pass, which is what the plot needs. |
| `analysis.frap` | enhance.temporal_gain corrects global bleaching - which is a preprocessing step for FRAP, not a substitute. analysis.reduce_scalar can produce a per-frame mean but cannot normalize or fit. |
| `analysis.fret` | none. |
| `analysis.front_kinematics` | analysis.extract_boundary gives the boundary per frame; util.zproject and analysis.measure give areas. Nothing tracks the boundary's own kinematics or spectrum across t. |
| `analysis.geodesic_distance` | analysis.edt is the unconstrained Euclidean transform; it cannot respect a mask's connectivity and has no marker input. analysis.boundary_band bands by Euclidean distance from a boundary. |
| `analysis.glcm` | none. |
| `analysis.granulometry` | none. detect.spots/detect.particles find puncta and analysis.measure sizes them, but both require successful detection; granulometry is detection-free. |
| `analysis.graph_partition` | analysis.segment watershed method (EDT-peak-seeded, on a binary mask). This consumes a continuous boundary map and needs no seeds; it is the only proposed route to instances that does not require either a peak-finding step or a trained instance head. |
| `analysis.hessian` | enhance.vesselness above consumes the same computation but emits one score; this emits the components. Keep both, or make this the 'raw' mode of that node - but the eigenvalue layers are worth having independently as classifier features. |
| `analysis.intensity_stats` | analysis.measure — this IS that node with more entries in _MEASURE_COLUMNS. |
| `analysis.iterative_threshold` | analysis.threshold / histogram_threshold / multiotsu all pick one or a few global levels; analysis.threshold_local varies the level spatially but not per object. flow.iterate sweeps a parameter but selects one global winner, not a per-object winner. |
| `analysis.joint_histogram` | The spreadsheet can export per-element values but there is no binned 2D density. Shares its guts with analysis.colocalize's cytofluorogram; build once. |
| `analysis.kymograph` | none. This is also the strongest argument for the meta_transform system: the output's axes are not the input's axes. |
| `analysis.kymograph_trace` | Conceptually a tracker, but on a (distance, t) image where a track is a straight line -- none of the existing linkers apply. No overlap. |
| `analysis.line_profile` | analysis.kymograph is the same sampling core taken across time and stacked into an image; this is the single-frame curve. Build the arclength/perpendicular sampler once and share it between this, kymograph and straighten. |
| `analysis.local_extrema` | detect.spots and detect.particles are LoG/DoG scale-space detectors (confirmed: enhance.remove_blobs offers method=log |
| `analysis.local_thickness` | analysis.edt is distance-to-boundary, which is a different quantity and is systematically smaller. The inscribed-ball radius in analysis.geodesic_metrics is one number per label; this is a per-voxel field. |
| `analysis.merge_by_distance` | analysis.cluster_points clusters with GaussianMixture/KMeans and a BIC model-order sweep -- a statistical grouping that needs a cluster count and is not deterministic. This is a purely geometric epsilon-merge with no model, which is what deduplication requires (a GMM will happily merge two genuinely distinct spots if that improves BIC). |
| `analysis.merge_regions` | None. analysis.segment can only SPLIT; enhance.remove_blobs and the size filter can only DELETE. Merging is absent. |
| `analysis.neighbors` | analysis.tessellate and analysis.voronoi build synthetic neighbourhoods from seed points; analysis.object_metrics has n_neighbors/neighbor_dist_mean but those are centroid-distance based on POINTs, not shared-boundary based on LABELs. The contact-length output has no equivalent. |
| `analysis.nematic_defects` | none. Depends on analysis.structure_tensor and on the doubled-angle convention established by analysis.polarity. |
| `analysis.network_topology` | Sits directly downstream of analysis.skeleton and could be a mode of it; separated because networkx is a heavier dependency and because the loop/lacuna analysis is a distinct algorithm from branch measurement. |
| `analysis.object_metrics` | analysis.object_metrics — this IS that node with more entries in _OBJECT_METRIC_COLUMNS, reusing the velocity that is already computed once and shared. |
| `analysis.optical_flow` | analysis.dic_correlate (pyALDIC) and analysis.dvc_field (ALDVC) solve subset correlation with a global regularizer and produce strain tensors; this produces displacement only, densely, from a variational/local-window formulation. It does NOT replace them — no strain tensor, no confidence, no validated FranckLab parity — but it covers the low-texture regime where they fail and is the natural companion to... |
| `analysis.order_parameter` | analysis.reduce_scalar reduces a layer to a scalar with ordinary statistics, which is exactly the wrong thing for a mod-180 angle. This is the correct reducer for orientation fields and would be a natural companion to it. |
| `analysis.piv` | analysis.dic_correlate is the vendored pyALDIC global augmented-Lagrangian FE solve — different algorithm class, far slower, needs a good speckle pattern, and produces no S2N. Genuinely different tool, not a mode; the two should be cross-validated against each other on the same pair. |
| `analysis.polarity_offset` | analysis.measure already computes centroids and intensity means but not the intensity-weighted centroid, so this is naturally an added metric group in analysis.measure (op_key analysis.measure, effort S) rather than a new node. |
| `analysis.porosity` | S2(r) overlaps analysis.autocorrelation's binary mode - pick one owner (porespy's validated implementation) and have the other call it. Otherwise no overlap. |
| `analysis.power_spectrum` | Shares FFT machinery with analysis.frc but answers a different question (single-image spectrum vs two-image correlation). Overlaps analysis.focus_score's blur intent; keep both because PSD slope needs no reference and is threshold-free. |
| `analysis.proximity` | analysis.object_metrics 'neighbors' gives mean/std of the k nearest CENTROID distances within one layer, no partner id, no border modes, no cross-layer pairing. analysis.edt gives a distance FIELD to a mask but never attributes it to an object pair. |
| `analysis.radial_profile` | analysis.boundary_band and the EVF node above give distance-banded masks; analysis.reduce_scalar reduces to one number. Neither produces an intensity-versus-radius curve. Complements EVF: EVF is shape-normalized depth, this is metric radius. |
| `analysis.refine_contour` | analysis.threshold_local adapts the threshold per neighbourhood but is still a per-voxel decision with no surface-smoothness term; enhance.morphology can only dilate/erode uniformly. |
| `analysis.relabel` | analysis.label creates a table from a mask from scratch, discarding any existing ids. This preserves and reorganizes an existing table, which is the whole point. |
| `analysis.ripley` | analysis.object_metrics 'neighbors' gives k-NN distances per object; analysis.cluster_points assigns cluster ids by GMM/KMeans with a BIC model-order sweep. Neither gives a scale-resolved second-order statistic or a randomization envelope, so neither can say 'clustered at 2 µm, random above 6 µm'. |
| `analysis.roi_shapes` | analysis.roi_mask — this IS that node with more shape kinds in its serializable list. The 'multiple circles from a table' mode overlaps transform.grow_points (points to discs) but takes radii per row from a column rather than one global radius. |
| `analysis.seeded_grow` | analysis.segment method='watershed' does distance-transform watershed from an image. This operates on an already-existing mask/label layer, adds intensity-driven h-extrema splitting, and exposes the split lines as their own layer. |
| `analysis.segment.cellpose` | analysis.segment (stardist/cellsam methods). Differs from stardist: no star-convexity restriction, trained for cytoplasm not nuclei. Differs from cellsam: cellsam is 2D-only here (_SEGMENT_2D_ONLY) and prompt-free; Cellpose has a real 3D route and a calibrated diameter param. |
| `analysis.segment.instanseg` | analysis.segment. Unique vs stardist/cellsam: channel-invariant multichannel input and paired nuclei+cell output. 2D only, so it does not replace a 3D method. |
| `analysis.segment.voronoi_otsu` | analysis.segment threshold+watershed already computes seeds from the EDT with a single min_distance. This is that node with one more mode: seeds from a blurred-intensity maximum rather than from a distance-transform peak, with the outline blur decoupled. |
| `analysis.shape_descriptors` | analysis.measure — this IS that node with more entries in _MEASURE_SHAPE. min_feret and convexity need real code (skimage exposes only feret_diameter_max); the rest are one dict line each. |
| `analysis.shells` | analysis.boundary_band produces ONE outward band per region. This adds inward shells, a gap, multiple graded rings with an index column, and the fixed-radius disc mode — i.e. a radial profile rather than a single annulus. |
| `analysis.skeleton_metrics` | none. transform.label_to_points reduces labels to centroids; nothing traverses a curvilinear structure. |
| `analysis.skeletonize` | none. analysis.edt gives distance to a mask boundary but never thins; analysis.extract_boundary gives the outline, which is the opposite object. |
| `analysis.spatial_correlation` | Reads the same DIC/DVC fields analysis.accumulate_field consumes, but produces a correlation, not an accumulated displacement. No overlap. |
| `analysis.split_objects` | analysis.segment's watershed splits touching objects using image evidence; this splits ONE object geometrically and deterministically, which watershed cannot do. |
| `analysis.spot_quality` | analysis.measure computes mean/max/min/sum/median INSIDE the object via the voxel->label bridge; it has no background-annulus concept, so it cannot express either ratio. Could instead be two new `stats` values on measure (effort S) if annulus geometry is acceptable there. |
| `analysis.structure_tensor` | none - verified no structure_tensor reference in nodegraph/. The 'no rotation socket, no matrix socket' engine constraint is exactly why the tensor must be surfaced as separate scalar layers rather than a tensor socket. |
| `analysis.threshold_local_methods` | analysis.threshold_local exists; this is that node with the method menu filled out and a 3D spherical neighbourhood. |
| `analysis.threshold_methods` | analysis.histogram_threshold and analysis.multiotsu already exist; this is the same node with the method menu filled out. Deliberately scoped as 'the existing node with more modes'. |
| `analysis.topology` | analysis.measure exists; this is that node with a 3D metric family added. Phrased as a modification. transform.rasterize_mesh goes mesh->raster; the marching-cubes surface here is measured, not necessarily materialized as a MESH (though transform.transfer_structure's LABEL->MESH bridge may already do that - worth checking before scoping). |
| `analysis.zones_of_influence` | analysis.voronoi tessellates from seed POINTS (nearest-seed in µm), so territory boundaries are perpendicular bisectors of centroids. This uses object SHAPE, so a large cell claims more than an equidistant split; results differ whenever objects are not point-like. |
| `channel.unmix` | channel.split / merge / select move channels without arithmetic. analysis.image_math above could express a hand-rolled two-channel subtraction, but not a least-squares inversion of an n-channel mixing matrix with non-negativity. |
| `core.annotation_layer` | Picking params off the image yields scalars, not painted regions; analysis.roi_mask consumes a region but nothing authors a multi-class sparse one. Prerequisite for analysis.classify_pixels, analysis.classify_objects and (as ground truth) analysis.label_overlap. |
| `core.batch_files` | The m (MULTIPOINT) axis handles positions within one file; io.dock bakes one chain. Neither iterates over files or carries a condition table. |
| `core.categorical_column` | none. Complements `InString` (a param socket carrying one literal) — this is a per-element column, which no existing type can hold. |
| `core.cost_field` | field.py's Input/BinOp/Attr machinery is already right; the gap is a domain whose elements are pairs, and two-endpoint binding. |
| `core.curve_geometry` | analysis.extract_boundary produces boundary POINTS (unordered, no topology) and track.link/track.objects produce a TrackMembership (occupancy rows, no geometry). Neither carries the ORDER that makes a contour or a trajectory a curve, so arc-length, tangent and trimming are all unavailable. |
| `core.field_wire` | None. `field=True` inputs exist across the catalog and `OutValue(field=True)` exists in registry.py, but only flow.iterate emits a value output and it is a driver port, not a field expression. |
| `core.figure_layer` | `view.overlay` (draws INTO the acquisition raster at its own pixel grid, and its `output` mode is display/resample). A figure is not registerable to stage coordinates at all, so it cannot ride the overlay path. |
| `core.group_domain` | nodegraph/structure.py's StructureTable is close, but is keyed by object id and rides the DATASET wire as a layer of a specific domain. The GUI spreadsheet and CSV/Arrow export are read-only terminals, not a computable domain. |
| `core.group_interface` | group.input / group.output exist as interface markers and SocketSpec already carries description/unit/derive/choice_docs. Missing: panels, group-level enums, presets, and any library/versioning story. |
| `core.matrix_socket` | transform.rigid, registration.stabilize, align.drift and registration.align_to each carry their own internal notion of a transform, expressed as loose translate/rotate params. A matrix socket makes the transform a first-class value they can all exchange. |
| `core.mechanics_units` | The existing unit system covers um, um^2, um^3, nm and s. This is the same mechanism extended to force/stress/energy/rate, not a parallel system. |
| `core.missing_values` | `nodegraph/reducers.py::_has_nan` — the current implicit behaviour this replaces. |
| `core.node_ergonomics` | Labelled frames, reroutes and hot reload exist. Muting, per-node previews, the hover value readout, lazy connect and detach do not. |
| `core.onnx_backend` | None. It is the cheapest possible answer to 'how do we run the torch half of the zoo without adopting torch'. |
| `core.ortho_view` | util.zproject collapses z (max/mean/sum/min/median) and view.viewer displays a plane. Flagged: I did not read the nodelab_v2 GUI source, so if an orthoslice viewer already exists only the twin/comparison mode is new. |
| `core.overlay_render` | analysis.extract_boundary already produces the boundary VOXELS (find_boundaries' job); this is the RENDER and the per-object text annotation, which the existing overlay/placement system is the natural home for. transform.rasterize_field can paint a field into an image but has no per-object text and no outline compositing. |
| `core.pair_domain` | MESH is described as the only domain carrying topology, and it does so via CSR strata in nodegraph/mesh.py - so the storage pattern exists but is bound to mesh geometry. The 15 ordered structure-bridge pairs in transform.transfer_structure would gain a new participant. |
| `core.plot_panel` | The existing spreadsheet plus CSV/Arrow export shows and exports numbers; view.overlay draws images. There is no chart and no plot-to-image selection. |
| `core.profile_domain` | The existing spreadsheet plus CSV/Arrow export handles per-element scalar tables. Neither the socket types (FLOAT/INT/BOOL/VECTOR/COLOR/STRING/MENU) nor the eleven domains can carry a variable-length binned distribution attached to an element. This is the single highest-leverage architectural addition in the survey. |
| `core.pyramid` | util.resample rescales a dataset once as a graph step; io.dock bakes a chain to disk. Neither gives the Dataset multiple co-registered levels nor lets a node choose one. |
| `core.reference_state` | analysis.dvc_field/dic_correlate implement this privately for two nodes; nodegraph/catalog/_shared/dvc.py is the seed of the shared version. Promoting it is a refactor plus a contract, not a new behaviour for the existing two. |
| `core.row_subset` | none. `nodegraph.structure._canonical_relabel` does the raster half for freshly-labelled masks only. |
| `core.socket_model` | None — this is additive to the socket type list. Note it deliberately does NOT become a field: there is no meaningful per-element model. |
| `core.spreadsheet_sync` | A spreadsheet with CSV/Arrow export exists. Missing: row filters bound to a field, the domain switcher, multi-item viewer inspection, and any link between a table row and the pixels it describes. |
| `core.tensor_layers` | nodegraph/field.py already has Const/Attr/Input/BinOp/UnaryOp/Where over scalars and vectors; there is no matrix socket and no tensor concept. This adds the tensor layer/IR without adding a socket type, which is the cheapest form the engine facts allow. |
| `core.tiled_inference` | analysis.segment already hand-rolls per-frame chunking and per-worker model singletons; enhance.zs_deconvnet hand-rolls its own. This is the shared version those two should be refactored onto. |
| `core.track_display` | view.overlay is Dataset-vs-Dataset compositing and changes no data; it is not an annotation layer. The picking system currently picks PARAMETERS off the image, not DATA ELEMENTS off it. |
| `core.training_io` | io.dock bakes a chain to disk and the spreadsheet exports CSV/Arrow, but neither writes an annotated patch set with a training manifest, and nothing reads external ground truth into the structure domains. |
| `core.zone_blockwise` | zone.repeat and zone.frame iterate over a parameter and over t; neither partitions space. Granularity/kernel_axes describe the footprint but nothing tiles it. |
| `detect.refine_subpixel` | This IS detect.particles / detect.spots with a `refine` Mode and extra output columns. If detect.spots already does LoG subpixel interpolation, the added value narrows to the mass/size/ecc/ep column family and effort drops to S -- check both nodes' emitted columns first. |
| `detect.spots.spotiflow` | detect.spots (log/dog) and detect.particles. Same POINT output contract, so it drops into the existing node as a third method; what it adds is subpixel accuracy, threshold-independence, native 3D, and no σ guessing. |
| `enhance.anisotropic_diffusion` | enhance.tv_denoise minimizes total variation (isotropic, edge-preserving but not orientation-following); enhance.bilateral and enhance.nlm are patch/range filters. None uses a diffusion tensor, so none can smooth along a fibre. |
| `enhance.attenuation` | enhance.flatten_field corrects xy illumination; enhance.temporal_gain corrects bleaching over t. Neither has a z model, and applying flatten_field per plane would absorb genuine biological z structure into the correction. |
| `enhance.attribute_filter` | enhance.morphology and enhance.tophat use a structuring element; enhance.remove_blobs is a LoG/DoG blob detector that zeros detected blobs. None of the three performs a connected-component attribute criterion, so none is shape-preserving. Verified absent: no area_opening/max-tree call anywhere in nodegraph/. |
| `enhance.background` | enhance.flatten_field subtracts a large-σ Gaussian; enhance.tophat does a morphological white top-hat (which is the closest relative, being a flat structuring element rather than a rolling sphere). Adds the non-flat sphere/paraboloid kernel and the µm-calibrated radius, and gives literature-comparable numbers. |
| `enhance.calibrate_denoiser` | flow.iterate performs the search; this supplies the ground-truth-free objective that makes the search meaningful for denoising. No existing node evaluates image quality without a reference. |
| `enhance.care` | enhance.n2v and enhance.zs_deconvnet are both self-supervised; this is the supervised upper bound and the reference against which those two should be judged. Nothing in the catalog currently accepts a paired training target at all. |
| `enhance.care_isotropic` | util.resample changes sampling by interpolation and adds no information; enhance.deconvolve (Richardson-Lucy) and enhance.zs_deconvnet sharpen but do not learn an axial prior from the lateral planes, and neither rewrites the z spacing. This is the only candidate that addresses axial anisotropy as such. NOTE: lever is 'no' because the operation is 3D by definition. |
| `enhance.care_projection` | util.zproject — same axis reduction, but content-aware and with a height-map second output. Ship the classical focus-measure mode as a Mode of util.zproject (effort S) and the learned mode as this node. |
| `enhance.convolve` | enhance.gaussian, enhance.dog, enhance.unsharp and enhance.morphological_gradient are all fixed kernels; enhance.deconvolve derives a PSF from optics metadata. None accepts a user kernel, so a measured PSF or a template cannot be used at all. |
| `enhance.deconvolve.wiener` | enhance.deconvolve is Richardson-Lucy only. Same PSF machinery, different inversion — a mode addition, not a node. |
| `enhance.dic_integrate` | None — and note the NAME COLLISION: in this catalog 'DIC' means digital image correlation (analysis.dic_correlate, pyALDIC), whereas CellProfiler's E_DIC means differential interference contrast. The node label and docstring must disambiguate explicitly or users will wire it into a strain graph. |
| `enhance.edges` | enhance.morphological_gradient is the morphological analogue only — no orientation, no sub-pixel gradient, no Canny hysteresis, and unusable as a watershed cost surface at small radii. |
| `enhance.frequency` | enhance.dog approximates a spatial-domain bandpass but cannot suppress a directional stripe family or a notch frequency, and its cutoffs are not independently controllable. enhance.flatten_field removes a smooth illumination model, not periodic artifacts. |
| `enhance.hot_pixel` | enhance.median filters everything; enhance.remove_blobs detects LoG/DoG blobs with a min_radius default of 0.3 µm and repaints them. Neither is a per-voxel outlier test and neither builds a persistent defect map. NB the across-t defect-map mode is WHOLE_SERIES. |
| `enhance.inpaint` | enhance.remove_blobs actions zero/interpolate/median fill blobs its own LoG/DoG detector found. This takes an arbitrary mask and uses a fill that matches the surrounding gradient, which is what a correlation-based measurement requires. |
| `enhance.local_statistic` | enhance.median exists as a denoiser; enhance.morphological_gradient is the max-minus-min special case. Neither exposes variance, entropy or percentile as a measured output layer. |
| `enhance.n2v` | The nine classical enhance.* denoisers, all of which are hand-parameterised and content-agnostic. Also overlaps enhance.zs_deconvnet, which is also self-supervised and trains in-node — but ZS-DeconvNet's objective is deconvolution with a PSF, needs the optics, and can over-sharpen; N2V is pure denoising with no PSF and no resolution claim, which makes it the safe default and ZS-DeconvNet the aggressive option. |
| `enhance.noise_model` | None. enhance.normalize rescales intensity; nothing in the catalog characterises the sensor. It also retires two hand-guessed parameters that enhance.zs_deconvnet currently exposes. |
| `enhance.reconstruct` | enhance.morphology does flat open/close only; enhance.tophat does the SE-based top-hat. Neither can propagate a marker under a mask. Verified absent: no `reconstruction(` call in nodegraph/. |
| `enhance.ridge` | enhance.dog is an isotropic band-pass; enhance.morphological_gradient is a first-order edge operator. Neither uses second-order (Hessian) shape information, so neither can distinguish a tube from an edge or a blob. Verified absent: no frangi/sato/meijering in nodegraph/. |
| `enhance.shading` | enhance.flatten_field removes a per-plane large-σ Gaussian background — an ADDITIVE, per-plane correction estimated from each plane in isolation. Shading is MULTIPLICATIVE and shared across the whole acquisition; applying flatten_field per plane cannot recover it and destroys real large-scale intensity structure while trying. |
| `field.operator` | enhance.morphological_gradient and enhance.dog are image filters with no physical units; object_field hard-codes div/curl for one specific input. This is the reusable, unit-correct operator, and it is what makes the field IR usable for mechanics. |
| `flow.bake` | io.dock (GUI-side bake-to-disk) does the same I/O. This makes it a first-class node so a graph can have several independently-invalidated bake points, which a single GUI-level dock boundary cannot express. |
| `flow.switch` | flow.iterate sweeps a parameter and searches; zone.repeat and zone.sim iterate unconditionally. Nothing chooses between two subgraphs, and nothing skips work. |
| `io.file_output` | io.dock bakes a chain to disk for REUSE (a cache keyed by the recipe hash) and the spreadsheet exports CSV/Arrow. Neither publishes a named, templated, multi-artifact result tree, which is a different job with different naming requirements. |
| `io.load_bioformats` | io.load exists (GUI-side, ND2-focused). This is that node with a format-plugin layer. Flag the JVM dependency explicitly - it is the reason to consider pure-Python per-format readers first. |
| `io.load_table` | none. The catalog exports CSV/Arrow but imports nothing except image files. |
| `io.rois` | analysis.roi_mask presumably builds a mask from an ROI defined inside the app; there is no interchange with ImageJ ROI files in either direction. The CSV/Arrow export covers tables, not geometry. |
| `io.tracks` | The existing CSV/Arrow export dumps table columns flat; it cannot express a lineage forest or the CTC label-TIFF pairing, and cannot read anything back. |
| `io.write_mesh` | `transform.rasterize_mesh` (MESH -> VOXEL, in-graph). No node writes MESH to a file. |
| `io.write_movie` | `view.overlay` (draws annotations into the raster for on-screen display; no encoder, no scale bar, no clock, no file output) |
| `io.write_ome_zarr` | `io.dock` (bake-to-disk checkpoint — a private .npy/memmap format, per nodegraph/checkpoint.py, for resuming a graph, not for interchange) |
| `io.write_report` | none. Requires core.figure_artifact and core.multi_input_dataset. |
| `io.write_table` | the existing GUI CSV/Arrow export (`nodelab_v2/export.py`) — same code path, but as a node it becomes schedulable, sweepable and headless. This is the clearest 'wrap what exists' card in the survey. |
| `io.write_tiff` | none. Folds OME-TIFF, ImageJ-TIFF and label-mask export into one node with a Mode because tifffile is one call with different metadata. |
| `mask.fill_holes` | analysis.segment has a fill_holes bool that applies only to that node's own output; this makes it a standalone step with a size limit and a 3D lever. |
| `mech.d2min` | analysis.object_metrics computes per-object local divergence/curl from neighbour relative velocities — the affine part only, as two scalars. D2min is the residual after removing the affine part, which object_metrics discards. |
| `mech.deformation_gradient` | analysis.dvc_field / analysis.dic_correlate produce a grid displacement field and analysis.accumulate_field integrates one over t -- all Eulerian. analysis.object_metrics' divergence/curl are first-order VELOCITY gradients over k-NN, in 1/s; this is the finite-strain DISPLACEMENT gradient, dimensionless, with a reference configuration. Related maths, different quantity. |
| `mech.detrend` | align.drift and registration.stabilize estimate and remove drift from the RASTER. This works on the structure table, runs after tracking, costs nothing, and is the only option once a tracked chain has been docked. |
| `mech.field_alignment` | none. The field IR can do arithmetic on scalars but has no notion of an axis convention, of tensor principal directions, or of circular statistics. |
| `mech.fill_field` | transform.rasterize_field interpolates Point columns onto the FULL voxel grid (griddata linear/nearest) for display. This repairs holes in place on the coarse Point grid, keeping the grid regular, and marks what it invented. |
| `mech.mesh_strain` | none. transform.rasterize_mesh and transform.transfer_structure move data to/from MESH; nodegraph/mesh.py has the topology but no kinematics. No node computes anything mechanical on a mesh. |
| `mech.microrheology` | none. Note this is PASSIVE microrheology; active microrheology needs a driven probe (optical/magnetic tweezers), which is a hardware capability the graph cannot supply — the node should say so in its docs rather than offer a fake 'active' mode. |
| `mech.monolayer_stress` | none — there is no stress node. Consumes mech.traction_fttc output; feeds mech.line_tension, mech.field_alignment and mech.strain_invariants. |
| `mech.pillar_force` | detect.particles finds the pillar tops today but stops there; nothing knows about a reference lattice or a bending stiffness. Shares the lattice-fitting kernel with mech.traction_reference_free. |
| `mech.polar_decompose` | none — nothing in the repo decomposes a tensor. registration.stabilize/transform.rigid remove a GLOBAL rigid motion from the image; this measures LOCAL rotation per subset and keeps it as data. |
| `mech.resample_field` | transform.rasterize_field interpolates Point columns to the FULL VOXEL grid with scipy.griddata linear/nearest and nearest-fill outside the hull — a display raster whose derivative is unusable, and whose output domain is wrong for FTTC/strain. This produces a coarse REGULAR POINT grid with a smoothing length and a support mask. |
| `mech.smooth_field` | analysis.dic_correlate exposes disp_smoothness/strain_smoothness and analysis.dvc_field exposes strain_smooth — solver-internal regularizers, not reusable and not GCV-automatic. enhance.gaussian/tv_denoise smooth IMAGES on the lattice, not a Point-domain vector field with gaps. |
| `mech.strain_energy` | none. analysis.measure and analysis.object_metrics work on intensity/shape columns; neither can contract two vector fields against each other over an object. |
| `mech.strain_invariants` | none. analysis.reduce_scalar reduces an existing column to a scalar; it cannot form an invariant from a tensor's components. |
| `mech.strain_measure` | analysis.dvc_field has a strain_type Mode with exactly two choices (infinitesimal / finite). This is that Mode generalized to four measures and detached from the correlation, so a PIV or track-derived F can be converted too; if it were folded in instead, it would be analysis.dvc_field with two more Mode choices (effort S). |
| `mech.traction_25d` | mech.traction_fttc is the 2-component in-plane-only case. This is not a mode of it: it needs a genuinely 3D input field, a different kernel, and a z-depth-weighted inversion. |
| `mech.traction_3d` | none. It is also the strongest answer to 'how do strain fields relate to the MESH domain': the mesh is the inversion's boundary, and traction lives on its facets. |
| `mech.traction_fttc` | none — the catalog has no force or stress node of any kind. It consumes analysis.dic_correlate / analysis.piv output, so it is strictly downstream, not a duplicate. |
| `mech.traction_metrics` | analysis.measure and analysis.object_metrics measure intensity/shape/velocity per object; neither can integrate a vector field with force units over an object, and neither has the front/rear polarity notion that needs the track direction. |
| `mech.traction_regularization` | none. It is deliberately a separate value-producing node so flow.iterate's golden/secant search can sweep it and so one lambda can be pinned across a whole plate — which a lambda buried in the FTTC node's params cannot express. |
| `mech.validate_field` | analysis.dvc_field's repair_zncc/cc_thresh reject by absolute correlation only, per subset, before the field exists. This is the field-level, neighbour-relative test that catches a high-ZNCC vector pointing the wrong way — the failure mode ZNCC cannot see. |
| `mech.velocity_gradient` | analysis.object_field emits `divergence` and `curl` (1/s) but ONLY from tracked LABEL/POINT object velocities binned to a grid, 2D only, and only those two scalars. This works on any continuum vector field (DIC/DVC/PIV/optical flow), in 3D, and gives the full tensor plus the shear/rotation classification. |
| `mesh.contact` | analysis.boundary_band makes an outward voxel band per label (an unsigned proximity mask); it cannot say which pair of objects contact each other, nor give an area in µm² on a smoothed surface. |
| `mesh.decimate` | none. |
| `mesh.neighbors` | analysis.voronoi builds territory regions and analysis.tessellate builds per-label surfaces, but neither exposes the ADJACENCY between regions as a queryable field -- so the number of neighbours per cell is not obtainable. |
| `mesh.ops` | analysis.tessellate builds meshes and transform.rasterize_mesh converts back to voxels. Nothing operates ON a mesh once built -- there is no mesh-editing family at all, so the MESH domain is currently a terminal output. |
| `mesh.raycast` | transform.rasterize_mesh already does an interior test 'inherited from the mesh provenance (convex hull, or faces-based parity)'. That parity test is the machinery a raycast needs; this exposes it as a general directional query with hit position, normal, distance and attribute interpolation. |
| `mesh.shortest_paths` | analysis.edt gives a straight-line um distance transform on the voxel lattice. Geodesic distance is a different quantity and there is no way to get it today. |
| `mesh.signed_distance` | analysis.edt is a mask-based unsigned EDT quantized to the voxel lattice, with no per-object identity and no signed interior; boundary_band is a binary band. Neither can express 'signed distance to the nearest of 400 nuclear surfaces, subvoxel'. |
| `mesh.smooth` | none. analysis.tessellate produces MESH but has no post-processing; nodegraph/mesh.py has surface_area_um2/enclosed_volume_um3 but no vertex-moving operation. |
| `model.confidence` | None directly. It is the piece that makes analysis.object_classify able to reject DL artifacts and the piece that lets analysis.measure weight objects by trust. Should be built as part of the tiled-inference capability rather than bolted on. |
| `model.object_classifier` | analysis.cluster_points clusters POINTs spatially (DBSCAN-like); this clusters or classifies LABELs in an arbitrary feature space and has a supervised path. Distinct problems. |
| `model.pixel_classifier` | analysis.segment's stardist and cellsam modes are pretrained inference with no training path. This is orthogonal. Depends on the sparse-annotation core-capability below; without a brush the node has no training input. |
| `model.prompt_segment` | analysis.segment cellsam method (a SAM ViT-B, but prompted internally by CellFinder and 2D-only). This node's unique capability is a user/data-driven prompt socket and mask propagation through z and t; neither is reachable from analysis.segment's contract. |
| `model.qc` | None. nodegraph.selftest checks the app's code; this checks a third party's weights, which is a different failure surface entirely. |
| `model.run` | None. enhance.zs_deconvnet and analysis.segment's stardist/cellsam methods are hand-wired single models; this is the generic substrate they should eventually share. |
| `model.virtual_stain` | channel.merge/select/split move channels around; nothing synthesises one. Distinct from every restoration node: those improve an existing measurement, this fabricates a channel — which is exactly why the output must be metadata-flagged as predicted and paired with analysis.squirrel or analysis.image_metrics before anyone quantifies it. |
| `plot.categorical` | none. Folds GA3's separate Barchart / Boxplot / Violin into one node with a Mode, since the data contract is identical. Requires core.figure_artifact. |
| `plot.gallery` | `util.crop` (one region, changes the Dataset's axes and metadata). A gallery makes a display artefact from thousands of crops without touching the Dataset geometry. |
| `plot.line` | none. Requires core.figure_artifact. |
| `plot.scatter` | none. Requires core.figure_artifact. |
| `qc.assay` | `analysis.reduce_scalar` writes Global scalars for `flow.iterate` to compare, but only single reducers. Z' is a two-population separability statistic it cannot express. |
| `qc.coregistration_error` | Downstream of analysis.psf_metrics (shares the bead fitting) and the validation counterpart of registration.chromatic. Could be a mode of psf_metrics at effort S. |
| `qc.focus_score` | none. The PLANE{m,t,z} domain exists and has almost no producers; this is a natural one. |
| `qc.force_balance` | none. analysis.reduce_scalar could sum one column but has no concept of a vector sum over a mask, nor of the moment integral. |
| `qc.gate` | Overlaps value.math for the predicate itself and analysis.classify_objects for the binning idea — the distinct part is the lattice domain and the exclusion semantics. |
| `qc.illumination_uniformity` | enhance.flatten_field estimates a shading field in order to divide it out. This node reports the metrics of that field as QC numbers with the MetroloJ definitions and does not modify the data - it could reasonably be a report-only output mode on enhance.flatten_field (effort S). |
| `qc.image_quality` | registration.stabilize and align.drift detect frame-to-frame SHIFT and correct it; neither scores quality nor produces an exclusion flag. No node writes to the FRAME domain today. |
| `qc.image_similarity` | None. Nothing in the catalog assesses whether an enhancement was faithful. It should arguably be wired as a default companion output for enhance.zs_deconvnet, enhance.dfcan and enhance.srrf. |
| `qc.label_overlap` | none. |
| `qc.noise_floor` | none. analysis.dvc_field can be pointed at a ref/ref pair by hand, but nothing computes the floor statistics or propagates them into strain units, and nothing records them as metadata a downstream node can threshold against. |
| `qc.pattern_quality` | detect.particles/detect.spots find the beads; nothing turns bead statistics into a per-subset correlation-feasibility judgement tied to the correlation node's subset_size. |
| `qc.peak_lock` | none — no QC diagnostics exist for the correlation nodes beyond the per-subset qfactor column. |
