### F.3 Real blockers named (38)

| Candidate | Blocker |
|---|---|
| `analysis.glcm` | mahotas not installed (verified); only the skimage subset is dep-free |
| `analysis.kymograph` | per-axis (y,x) pixel_size_um is an explicit deferral in CALIBRATION_KEYS — only bites the t-collapsed encoding |
| `analysis.network_topology` | skan not installed; networkx-only branch walk must be written by hand |
| `analysis.optical_flow` | torch (StrainNet sub-variant only) — not installed, no CUDA |
| `analysis.piv` | openpiv not installed; no scipy-only fallback declared |
| `analysis.porosity` | porespy not installed and declared as a hard dep |
| `analysis.segment.cellpose` | torch — not a dependency, CPU-only Windows, no CUDA |
| `analysis.segment.instanseg` | torch — TorchScript still needs the torch runtime; not installed, no CUDA |
| `core.figure_layer` | shape_for(GLOBAL) == () so a GLOBAL layer is a scalar — but the Dataset image slot has no such limit |
| `core.pyramid` | a new NodeSpec/define_node field is required (registry.py:986-1013 accepts a closed kwarg list) |
| `core.tensor_layers` | with_attribute admits no trailing component axis (dataset.py:200-210), so tensors must be column groups — as the row says |
| `detect.spots.spotiflow` | torch: not installed, no CUDA on this machine, and a second DL runtime alongside the existing TF/Keras stack |
| `enhance.n2v` | the `n2v` package (absent, Keras-2 era) and careamics/torch (absent); only a repo-side implementation on csbdeep/Keras 3 is available |
| `io.file_output` | socket lists are static, so slot count must be fixed; and a memo hit skips the side effect (engine.py:719-724) |
| `io.load_table` | none for storage; only reducers/field ops over string columns need work |
| `io.rois` | none architecturally; roifile is a new (uninstalled) dependency |
| `io.tracks` | a catalog root gets an empty MetaEnvelope (propagate_meta seeds roots from `seeds` only), so file INGEST must stay GUI-side |
| `io.write_movie` | none architecturally; imageio-ffmpeg is a new dependency |
| `io.write_ome_zarr` | ReadContext.freeze makes a ctx.calib call from inside a returned lazy closure a hard RuntimeError |
| `io.write_table` | a memo hit skips the write on an identical second pull |
| `io.write_tiff` | calibration must be resolved before any lazy closure (ReadContext.freeze) — a ctx.calib call inside a returned provider is a hard RuntimeError |
| `model.object_classifier` | PICK_KINDS has no object-select gesture, so in-viewer labelling is unavailable — the class column must be imported or rule-derived |
| `model.pixel_classifier` | roi_mask's shape list is frame-independent (broadcast to all m/t/z/c), so per-plane annotation is not expressible |
| `model.run` | static socket lists cap multi-input models; extra_layers must be total and is re-entered on every keystroke |
| `plot.gallery` | a catalog module may not import nodelab_v2 (import-hygiene gate), so the renderer must live in nodegraph/ or catalog/_shared/ |
| `plot.line` | none for a figure-as-Dataset; only vector/SVG output and crop-immunity need a new artifact kind |
| `qc.gate` | meta_transform cannot read pixels, so a dropped-element count is never statically knowable — the affected axis must go UNKNOWN |
| `registration.deformable` | itk-elastix / antspyx both absent; no vendored deformable optimizer exists to fall back on |
| `stats.curve_fit` | the lattice has no (m,z) or (m,z,c,y,x) member, so a per-voxel or per-plane fit has no 'domain minus t' target |
| `table.aggregate` | reducers.REDUCERS has no dispersion reducer, and std is not a tree-reducible partial (reducers.py:287-292) |
| `table.computed_column` | none; must ship alongside a Field producer or the input socket is unreachable |
| `table.filter` | none for the column form; the field form waits only on a producer NODE, not on any engine change |
| `table.manage_columns` | _layer_names_out has no removal declaration — the edit-time layer catalog cannot be shrunk by a node |
| `table.window` | no per-element structure write exists — the whole table is re-emitted, and z_kind reverts to 'subpixel' if not carried |
| `track.edit` | no nodelab_v2 track-edit gesture exists (GUI, not core) |
| `track.objects.magik` | deeptrack absent and Keras-2-pinned against a Keras-3 TensorFlow |
| `track.objects.ultrack` | ultrack + a MIP solver, none installed; torch-free claim unverified |
| `value.math` | _map_image handles one image provider only (map_image.py:65-67), so the two-thick-wire form cannot ride the tile path |

## Appendix G - Completeness findings (37)

What the ten blind surveys could not see about themselves. Read the verdict first; it is the
single most consequential piece of analysis in this exercise.

> The 269 candidates cover the ANALYSIS middle of the pipeline thoroughly and the two ends barely at all. Four systematic blind spots, none of which any single survey could have revealed: (1) **Nothing guards the numbers the app already prints.** The set is entirely additive. Absent from all 269 and all 205: refractive-index axial scaling (every µm³ in a live-cell aqueous sample is off by 0.7-1.3x; Diel/Lichtman/Richardson 2020 Nat Protoc), real per-frame timestamps (the repo's own selftest at selftest.py:11425 records that dropping them made "every velocity in the app" wrong, and the fix — a median dt_s — still discards the per-frame truth), z-clipped and border-touching objects, saturation inside the objects being measured, immersion n hardcoded to 1.5 at _shared/psf.py:43, and physically anisotropic DVC subsets (dvc_field.py:211 is `unit="px"` while voxel_size_um is anisotropic). Each of these outranks any new descriptor in the set. (2) **Nothing interrogates the source.** No acquisition completeness, stage-log consistency, timestamp-gap or Nyquist-sampling check. The set begins by assuming a good Dataset. (3) **The output half of the workflow is architecturally foreclosed by one unmade decision, made fifteen times.** "A compute must return a Dataset / there is no plot artifact / there is no mutate-in-place path" killed figures, reports, profiles, curation and proofreading independently. Two of those rejections are also factually stale (enhance.psf_distill's "the consumer socket does not exist" — zs_deconvnet has psf_path), and the human-in-the-loop one is right about ids and wrong about the conclusion: corrections keyed on (m,t,x_um,y_um,z_um) survive an upstream re-run by construction, which is how Ultrack and ilastik do it. (4) **Convergence is anti-correlated with fit.** Every conv>=4 item is generic plumbing every survey needed as a precondition; every domain-specific correctness item is conv 1, and so is `core.batch_files` on a plate-based lab. Four shipped nodes are genuine stubs: `align.drift` (88 lines, ZERO params, hardcoded mid-plane/channel-0/t=0 reference, and lateral-only — axial focus drift over a multi-hour 3D run is corrected by nothing in the app or the set), `enhance.wavelet_denoise` (43 lines, zero params), `analysis.threshold_local` (hardcoded gaussian method, 2D only, int64 mask for a boolean), and `analysis.roi_mask` (all drawn shapes collapse into ONE mask, so "draw N regions and trace each over time" is inexpressible). Two more are hollow in a shared way: `nodegraph/reducers.py` has no std/var/percentile at all, which silently limits `analysis.measure`, `analysis.reduce_scalar` and both Transfer nodes; and no thresholding node emits the level it chose, so the app's auto-derived decisions are unauditable. The best Blender analogue nobody proposed is a **per-position (m) zone** — there is one for t (`zone.frame`) and none for the acquisition's own outer axis, despite MULTIPOINT being one of the eleven domains. The shallowest treatment of the deepest idea is the **asset/preset library**, buried as a clause in `core.group_interface` at value 5, conv 1. 37 findings below: 17 additions, 16 deepenings of shipped nodes or under-scoped candidates, 4 table-level flags.

### G.1 Additions - absent from all 269 and all 205 (16)

These are new. None of them appears anywhere in the candidate pool or the rejections, and the top of the list outranks most of Appendix A.

| Target | v | Gap | What to do |
|---|---|---|---|
| `util.axial_scale` | 10 | Refractive-index mismatch axial scaling has no representation in the 269, the 205, or the 72. Every 3D µm³, sphericity, axial distance, EDT and DVC z-displacement the app reports is wrong by the focal-shift factor whenever the immersion RI and the specimen RI differ — which is always for live cells (aqueous, n≈1.33–1.38) under an oil or a high-NA objective. | Node: `util.axial_scale` (category utility/registration-adjacent). Inputs: the immersion medium (oil 1.518 / glycerol 1.47 / water 1.33 / air 1.0), the specimen medium RI, the objective NA, and an `nz_nominal` override; output is the SAME pixels with `z_step_um` rescaled by the depth-dependent focal-shift factor plus a stamped provenance key, so nothing resamples and every downstream µm_axial unit and volume follows automatically through `nodegraph/metadata.py`. Two backends worth offering as modes: the paraxial ratio n_spec/n_imm, and the NA-dependent high-aperture factor. Provenance: Diel, Lichtman & Richardson, 'Tutorial: avoiding and correcting sample-induced spherical aberration artifacts in 3D fluorescence microscopy', Nature Protocols 15:2773 (2020); Besseling, Jose & van Blaaderen, 'Methods to calibrate and scale axial distances in confocal microscopy as a function of refractive... |
| `value.frame_time` | 9 | The ND2's real per-frame timestamps are read and then destroyed. `nodelab_v2.ingest._dt_from_timestamps` collapses `frame_timestamps_s` into one median scalar `dt_s`, and every rate in the app divides by that scalar. No candidate in the 269 exposes real time. In a 49-position round-robin with autofocus retries, a stage error or a paused run, the median deliberately hides exactly the intervals that are wrong. | Node: `value.frame_time` / a metadata change — emit `time_s` as a FRAME-domain layer (per (m,t)) plus `dt_s_actual` as its difference, then have `analysis.object_metrics` (velocity, speed, divergence, curl — all declared µm/s and 1/s), `track.*`, `track.msd`, `analysis.bleach_rate` and `enhance.temporal_gain` consume the layer instead of the calibration scalar. The repo's own selftest at `nodegraph/selftest.py:11442` documents the failure mode verbatim: 'a single long gap moved dt_s — use the median of the differences, not the mean' — the median is the right choice for a scalar, but the right fix is to stop needing a scalar. Provenance: the `nd2` package (tlambert03) — `ND2File.events()` returns the NIS recorded-data table including 'Time [s]', and `ND2File.frame_metadata(i).channels[0].time.relativeTimeMs` gives the per-frame stamp; the OME analogue is `Plane/@DeltaT`. Backend: `nd2`... |
| `zone.position` | 9 | There is a zone for t (`zone.frame`) and none for m. On a 49-position plate every per-position decision — a threshold picked per well, a per-position drift reference, a per-position dock, resume-after-failure at position 31 — is inexpressible. This is the deepest Blender structural analogue the surveys missed: they proposed `zone.foreach` (over ELEMENTS, conv 1) and `core.zone_blockwise` (over TILES, conv 1) but nobody proposed the loop over the acquisition's own outer axis, despite MULTIPOINT being one of the... | Node pair: `zone.position_in` / `zone.position_out`, mirroring `zone.repeat_*` and reusing `zone.frame`'s lazy-view trick — `nodegraph/catalog/zone/frame.py`'s `_FrameView` slices t==1 by wrapping the provider; the m analogue is the same class with `replace(base.axes, m=1)`. Inside the body, iteration i yields position i, so a MULTIPOINT-domain field becomes a per-well parameter source (which is the honest version of the rejected `core.param_drivers`), `analysis.reduce_scalar` becomes a per-well score, and `flow.iterate` can tune per position instead of globally. It also gives the batch story a spine: per-position bake, per-position failure isolation. Provenance: Blender 4.3's For Each Geometry Element zone and the Repeat Zone this repo already mirrors; the microscopy analogue is Nikon NIS-Elements GA3's per-multipoint loop and CellProfiler's `Groups` module (group by... |
| `io.provenance` | 9 | There is no provenance or methods export, in the 269 or the 72. `io.write_report` (conv 1) is a figure report. Phase-contrast mode solves the sparse linear inverse of the phase-contrast imaging model (an Airy-plus-negative-ring kernel) with an L1/nonneg prior, turning haloed cells into a clean artifact-free density map that ordinary thresholding segments; DIC mode integrates the shear-direction derivative to recover optical density. ### G.2 Deepen a shipped node or an under-scoped candidate (17)

Verified stubs and hollow shared vocabularies. Cheaper than a new node and each one silently limits several others.

| Target | v | Gap | What to do |
|---|---|---|---|
| `core.annotation_layer` | 9 | Human-in-the-loop correction was rejected three times (`core.track_editing`, `core.node_tools`, and `track.edit` scored down to 5) on one argument: 'a replayable per-element edit list has no stable key in a pull-based graph, so recorded corrections cannot survive the upstream re-run they exist to survive'. The premise is right and the conclusion is wrong — the standard solution is not to key corrections on element ids. | Deepen `core.annotation_layer` (v9, conv 4 — already the highest-converged human-in-the-loop item) from 'sparse annotation + brush for training data' into the correction substrate: corrections keyed on PHYSICAL, upstream-invariant coordinates — (m, t, x_um, y_um, z_um) plus an operation (split here, merge these two, this is not a cell, link this to that) — which survive re-segmentation, a changed threshold, and a re-run exactly because they name a place in the specimen rather than a row in a table. That makes proofreading a first-class, diffable, serializable node param (the same trick `analysis.roi_mask` already uses for drawn shapes: JSON in a STRING socket with `pick_kind`), keeps the memo honest, and unlocks ground-truth annotation, curation and the training-data export (`core.training_io`) from one mechanism. Provenance: Ultrack accepts user annotations as hard linking constraints... |

This also retires the rejection of `enhance.psf_distill` ('the consumer socket it exists to feed does not exist'), which is now factually stale. Provenance: Gibson & Lanni, 'Experimental test of an analytical model of aberration in an oil-immersion objective lens used in... |
| `qc.gate` | 8 | `qc.gate` is scoped to 'element exclusion' — rows in a structure table. The half that salvages a real experiment is missing: excluding a LATTICE frame. An out-of-focus timepoint, a bubble that drifted over position 17, a dead well, the pre-bleach first frame — today there is no way to say 'these (m,t) frames are invalid' and have every downstream statistic, reducer and transfer honour it. The alternative is deleting the run. | Deepen `qc.gate` to a FRAME/PLANE/MULTIPOINT-domain boolean validity layer that reducers, `analysis.reduce_scalar`, both Transfer nodes and the plot/export path skip rather than average over — plus a producer path so the flags can come from `qc.focus_score`, `qc.image_quality`/saturation, `qc.acquisition_integrity` or a hand-picked frame set (the F9 frame-picking gesture already exists in the GUI, MANUAL §6). The distinction to keep sharp: masking values to NaN and having reducers ignore them (the reducers are already nan-aware, `nodegraph/reducers.py:_has_nan`) versus dropping frames from the axes — the former preserves the lattice and is almost certainly the right answer. Provenance: CellProfiler's `FlagImage` module (flags propagate and downstream modules can skip flagged images); Nikon NIS-Elements GA3's frame-rejection step; the general practice codified in QUAREP-LiMi's... |
| `core.batch_files` | 8 | Batch over files is convergence 1 and value 8 on an app whose stated workload is multipoint plates and whose real benchmark is a 49-position file — nine of ten surveys did not see it because none was assigned 'now run this on the whole study'. And as scoped it is only the fan-out; the parts that make a 200-file overnight run survivable are absent. | Deepen `core.batch_files` beyond 'iterate a study table': a run MANIFEST (which file, which graph version and hash, which node failed and why, wall time, output paths), RESUME from that manifest so a crash at file 137 does not re-run 136, per-file failure isolation (one bad file must not take the run down), and a study-level table that concatenates every file's results with its metadata columns already joined. Together with `zone.position` and `io.provenance` this is the difference between a tool and a pipeline. Provenance: CellProfiler's `CreateBatchFiles` + Analyst batch workflow (per-image-set batch files plus a resumable output database); Nikon NIS-Elements GA3's batch-over-folder recipe execution; snakemake/nextflow as the general manifest-and-resume pattern. Backend: json/sqlite + the existing headless entry point (MANUAL §17) and `nodegraph/parallel.py`. |
| `analysis.threshold_local` | 7 | Shipped stub, and the 269 propose replacing it with a sibling instead of fixing it. `analysis.threshold_local` (42 lines) hardcodes skimage's default `method='gaussian'` — no mean, median, niblack or sauvola, no `param` for the gaussian sigma — is 2D-only, and eagerly materializes `np.zeros((m,t,z,c,y,x), dtype=np.int64)` for a BOOLEAN mask (threshold_local.py:29): 8 bytes per voxel, which is 2.1 GiB on a 268-Mvoxel series, the exact waste `analysis.threshold` documents having already fixed ('2.0 GiB instead of... | The candidate `analysis.threshold_local_methods` (v6, conv 1) is mis-scoped as a new node — it is this node's missing Mode set. Deepen `analysis.threshold_local`: a `method` Mode (gaussian / mean / median / niblack / sauvola) with `param`/`k`/`r` gated per method, a 3D branch (block as a µm cube), the dtype fix, and lazy per-plane streaming through the same `_map_image` path the rest of the enhancement family uses. Provenance: `skimage.filters.threshold_local`, `threshold_niblack`, `threshold_sauvola`; Sauvola & Pietikäinen, 'Adaptive document image binarization', Pattern Recognition 33:225 (2000); Niblack, An Introduction to Digital Image Processing (1986); the menu is Fiji ▸ Image ▸ Adjust ▸ Auto Local Threshold (Landini's plugin), which is where the five-method vocabulary comes from. Backend: scikit-image, installed. |
| `registration.stabilize` | 7 | All motion correction in the app and in the 269 is either globally parametric (`registration.stabilize`: translation/euclidean/affine/feature) or fully deformable (`registration.deformable`, conv 2). The middle case that matters most for a live-cell field — the sample moves and DEFORMS locally, so one global transform is wrong everywhere while a dense deformable field is overkill and risks warping the biology being measured — has no representation. | Add a `piecewise` model to `registration.stabilize`: split the field into overlapping patches, estimate a rigid shift per patch by phase correlation, reject and interpolate low-confidence patches, then blend the patch shifts into a smooth displacement field with a strict cap on how much it may deform. Fits this node's existing 'register once on a reference channel, apply to all' contract and its `min_confidence` discipline. It is also the correct upstream for DVC/DIC in a way full deformable registration is not: it removes bulk sample motion without absorbing the strain the DVC node exists to measure — that boundary should be documented on the node. Provenance: NoRMCorre — Pnevmatikakis & Giovannucci, 'NoRMCorre: An online algorithm for piecewise rigid motion correction of calcium imaging data', J Neuroscience Methods 291:83 (2017), which is assay-agnostic despite its calcium framing;... |
| `enhance.shading` | 7 | The candidate is framed as reference-based FLAT-field correction only. The physically correct correction is (raw − dark)/(flat − dark): omit the dark/offset term and the correction is multiplicatively wrong everywhere, worst in the dim regions where granule intensities live. Neither the flat-only candidate nor `enhance.background` nor `enhance.flatten_field` (shipped) has an offset term, and no candidate proposes a retrospective darkfield estimate. | Deepen `enhance.shading` to estimate and apply BOTH terms, with a reference mode (a measured dark frame and a flat field acquired on the instrument) and a retrospective mode that estimates flatfield and darkfield jointly from the image collection itself — which is the mode this lab can actually use, since a 49-position plate IS the collection. Provenance: BaSiC — Peng, Thorn, Schroeder, Wang, Theis, Marr & Navab, 'A BaSiC tool for background and shading correction of optical microscopy images', Nature Communications 8:14836 (2017), which explicitly recovers flatfield AND darkfield with no calibration acquisition; backend `basicpy` (pure-Python/jax-optional port), or a direct low-rank + smoothness implementation on numpy/scipy if the dependency is unwanted. Correct order of operations relative to `enhance.flatten_field` (which is a spatial background estimator, not a detector... |
| `core.spreadsheet_sync` | 7 | Proposed at value 6, convergence 1, as 'row filters and selection sync' — i.e. a GUI nicety. The idea that makes it Blender-grade is absent: selection as DATA. If the current selection is a boolean field on its domain, then clicking objects in the viewer or brushing points in a plot becomes an input to the graph (filter to these, measure only these, exclude these) instead of a display state. | Deepen `core.spreadsheet_sync` to bidirectional selection-as-field: click a row → the object highlights in the viewer; click/lasso objects in the viewer → the rows highlight; and in both directions a `selected` boolean lands on the domain where `table.filter` (v10, conv 7) and the field wires can consume it. The persistence question has a clean answer already in this repo: store the selection the way `analysis.roi_mask` stores drawn shapes and the way the annotation-layer finding above stores corrections — physical coordinates in a serializable param — so it survives a re-pull and is diffable, which is what separates it from GUI state. Provenance: Blender's Spreadsheet editor selection sync with the 3D viewport plus its 'Selected Only' filter, and the `Selection` output on domain nodes that makes selection a field; napari's `Points.selected_data`; napari-clusters-plotter (Haase lab),... |
| `io.write_movie` | 7 | Publication figure furniture was rejected three separate times (`view.burn_in`, `view.montage`, `view.composite`, plus `view.temporal_color`) as 'presentation, not measurement'. The result is that this app — the only component in the chain that knows `pixel_size_um`, `dt_s`, the channel names and the µm origin — cannot put a correct scale bar or timestamp on anything, so that information gets re-entered by hand downstream, which is where scale-bar errors come from. | Deepen `io.write_movie` (and `io.write_report`) rather than adding a view node: burn in a calibrated scale bar, an elapsed-time stamp taken from the REAL per-frame timestamps (see `value.frame_time`), channel names with their LUTs, and the position/well identifier — all derived from metadata rather than typed by a user, which is precisely the argument for putting it here and not in a figure tool. This sidesteps every rejection reason (no new socket type, no new artifact class, no in-graph RGB layer) because the export path already produces pixels for a file. Provenance: Fiji ▸ Analyze ▸ Tools ▸ Scale Bar and ▸ Time Stamper as the feature baseline; the requirement is Schmied, Nelson, Avilov et al., 'Community-developed checklists for publishing images and image analyses', Nature Methods 21:170 (2024), whose figure checklist makes a calibrated scale bar and stated processing mandatory.... |
| `enhance.wavelet_denoise` | 6 | Shipped stub: `nodegraph/catalog/enhance/wavelet_denoise.py` is 43 lines and exposes NO parameters at all (the manual's Key controls column is literally '—'). It calls `denoise_wavelet(a, rescale_sigma=True)` with every knob at its skimage default: wavelet family, decomposition level, BayesShrink vs VisuShrink, soft vs hard thresholding, and an explicit sigma are all unreachable. It also silently passes a plane through when the backend returns non-finite values, which is honest but invisible. | Deepen with the four sockets that change the answer: `wavelet` (db1/db2/sym4/haar/coif1 — pywt's families), `method` (BayesShrink / VisuShrink), `mode` (soft / hard), `wavelet_levels`, and an optional `sigma` for when the noise level is known from `enhance.noise_model` rather than estimated. Report the sparse/flat-plane pass-through as a warning rather than swallowing it. Provenance: `skimage.restoration.denoise_wavelet`'s own signature; Chang, Yu & Vetterli, 'Adaptive wavelet thresholding for image denoising and compression', IEEE Trans Image Processing 9:1532 (2000) (BayesShrink); Donoho & Johnstone, Biometrika 81:425 (1994) (VisuShrink). Backend: `pywt` via scikit-image, already installed and already the declared dependency of this node. |

### G.3 Table-level flags (4)

Observations about the set as a whole.

| Target | v | Gap | What to do |
|---|---|---|---|
| `TABLE` | 10 | The 269 candidates are almost entirely ADDITIVE — new measurements, new descriptors, new solvers. Essentially none GUARD a number the app already prints. No survey was assigned 'what makes the µm, µm³, µm/s and 1/s figures this app already reports systematically wrong', so that entire tier is absent by construction, even though every item in it outranks any new descriptor: a corrected volume beats a new shape metric. | Concretely absent from all 269 and all 205: refractive-index axial scaling (every µm³ is off by 0.7–1.3×), real per-frame timestamps (every µm/s divides by a median dt_s), z-clipped and border-touching objects (volume bias in a stack thinner than the cells), saturation/clipping in the objects being measured, immersion n hardcoded to 1.5 in the PSF derivation (`nodegraph/catalog/_shared/psf.py:43`) for cells in aqueous media, and physically anisotropic DVC subsets. Each is individually cited below. The framing to adopt: a 'bias and calibration' category alongside `qc.*`, whose members change existing numbers rather than adding columns. Provenance for the tier as a whole: Jonkman, Brown, Wright, Anderson & North, 'Tutorial: guidance for quantitative confocal microscopy', Nature Protocols 15:1585 (2020); QUAREP-LiMi (Nelson et al., J Microsc 284:56, 2021). |
| `TABLE` | 9 | Roughly fifteen rejections are the SAME architectural decision made fifteen times: 'a compute must return a Dataset / the wire is DATASET-only / there is no plot artifact / there is no mutate-in-place path'. That killed view.chart, view.burn_in, view.montage, view.composite, view.object_gallery, view.temporal_color, analysis.line_profile (×2), analysis.profile_line, core.node_tools, core.track_editing, plot.heatmap, plot.lineage_tree, and more. Presented as fifteen independent judgements, it is one unmade... | The consequence: the entire OUTPUT half of a research workflow — figure, report, curation, annotation, profile — is unreachable, so the app can compute a publishable number but cannot produce the publishable figure or the correction that made the number right. Two candidates already gesture at the fix (`core.figure_layer` 'rendered plot artifact', `core.profile_domain`) at conv 1 and 3. The decision to take explicitly: does the socket enum gain a non-Dataset artifact branch (a FIGURE/TABLE payload), or is the boundary permanently at CSV/Arrow export? Either answer is fine; leaving it implicit means fifteen more rejections. Provenance for why it matters: Schmied, Nelson, Avilov et al., 'Community-developed checklists for publishing images and image analyses', Nature Methods 21:170 (2024) — a figure without a calibrated scale bar and stated processing is not publishable, and this app is... |
| `TABLE` | 8 | Convergence is being used as a priority signal but it measures survey overlap, not fit. Every conv≥4 item is generic plumbing (table.filter, value.math, table.aggregate, value.named_attribute) that every survey needed as a precondition for its own proposals; every domain-specific correctness item sits at conv 1. So convergence is anti-correlated with what is missing. | Ten blind surveys all needing a math node says the math node is a shared dependency, not that it is the highest-value work. Test: `core.batch_files` (batch over files / study table) is conv 1 on a lab whose real benchmark file is a 49-position plate and whose stated workflow is longitudinal — nine surveys missed it because none was assigned 'run this on 200 files'. Same for `core.reference_state`, `util.plate_map` (conv 2), `qc.plate_effect`. Recommend re-scoring conv-1 items in the acquisition/batch/provenance cluster upward and treating conv≥5 as 'prerequisite', a separate axis from value. |
| `TABLE` | 8 | Nothing in the 269 is about the SOURCE. The set begins by assuming a correct, complete, trustworthy Dataset already on the wire: the only io read candidates are load_table, load_bioformats and (rejected) read_ome_zarr. No survey was assigned acquisition-side integrity, so the file itself is never interrogated. | The repo already proves the hazard is real: `nodegraph/selftest.py:11425` records that `frame_timestamps_s` was read from every ND2 and dropped by `read_calibration` because 'dt_s is a CALIBRATION key and the timestamps are not', so 'every velocity in the app was' wrong until it was patched — and the patch was to take a MEDIAN, which still discards the per-frame truth. Source-side items missing entirely: acquisition completeness, stage-log self-consistency, per-position frame counts, timestamp monotonicity and gaps, the ND2 recorded-data/event table (`nd2.ND2File.events()`), and the PFS/Z focus log per timepoint (`nodegraph/placement.py:32` says `stage_z_um[m]` is per-POSITION nominal focus, with no t dependence, so focus that moves during a 14-hour run is invisible). |
